# KNOWN_GAPS.md — living ledger (ACIS)

Format — one entry per gap, newest first. Never delete entries; close them with Status.

```
## GAP-NNNN: <one-line title>
- Severity: critical | high | medium | low
- Likelihood: high | medium | low
- Status: open | mitigated | accepted-risk | closed
- Area: [DIR:...] or spec §
- Description: what can go wrong and how we know
- Justification: why this status (esp. for accepted-risk)
- Linked: commits / test names / halt-postmortems
```

> **Archive note (2026-07-17, owner-ratified surface-area reduction):** entries with
> `Status: closed` and historical audit-run banners are moved VERBATIM (never edited,
> never deleted) to `KNOWN_GAPS_CLOSED.md` when closed. This file is the LIVE ledger:
> open / mitigated / accepted-risk only. Ledger discipline is unchanged — new findings
> land here first; closure moves the entry to the archive with its closing note.

## GAP-P12D-01: OOS primary tick source is HistData, not Dukascopy — master §13 provenance substituted (owner-ratified)
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: medium
- Status: superseded (2026-09-11, same day — the owner then chose **Dukascopy's official S3 archive** as the primary, so the primary provenance is Dukascopy again and there is no substitution; HistData moved to the cross-check role — see GAP-P12D-02 / master v2.13. Text below kept as the record of why HistData was vetted.)
- Residual: none — no provenance deviation remains; the HistData↔Dukascopy cross-source check this entry demanded is spec law (master §13, ACIS-09 C-BT-9) and is scheduled in TODO.md's §14 data plan.
- Area: [DIR:tests/backtest] data acquisition, master §13 (v2.9), ACIS-09 §5
- Description: §13 names Dukascopy tick primary (TrueFX validator). Measured from the owner's residential link 2026-09-10/11: Dukascopy admits ~one instrument-day (~24 hourly requests), then imposes a penalty that outlasts 5/10/20/40-min cooldowns (datacenter/GCP IPs are hard-banned outright, 2026-07-31) → the 35,072 day-chunk plan would take years. HistData.com publishes the same universe as one ASCII tick zip per pair-month (1,344 files). Verified 2026-09-11: all 8 instruments cover 2012–2025 (XAUUSD from 2009, EURGBP from 2002); a live month (EURGBP 2012-01) parsed **1,197,028 bid/ask ticks, 0 crossed / 0 non-positive / 0 out-of-order / 0 duplicate, max reported gap 177 s**. Resolution is preserved (tick, bid+ask); the VENDOR changes: retail-aggregated feed, **no tick volume** (ACIS reads none — `volume` is only validated ≥0), timestamps in **fixed EST (UTC−5, no DST)**, converted in the reader. Its quote density drives `tick_velocity`, which NVMA's liquidity veto and the scalp read RELATIVE to the session median — self-consistent within the source, but the live venue's density differs (sharpens [[GAP-0001]]).
- Justification: OWNER DECISION (in-chat 2026-09-11, "HistData primary"). The only free route to the full ratified universe × 14 yr at tick resolution. **Binding condition:** before a §14 pass is presented, run the loader's `cross_source_drift` HistData-vs-Dukascopy on overlapping days (the Dukascopy job, newest-first, supplies them); any window over threshold is investigated, never averaged (C-BT-9). Per-file provenance (sha256, row/anomaly counts, HistData gap report) is recorded in `data/histdata/manifest.jsonl`.
- Linked: scripts/download_histdata.py, archive/download_dukascopy_publicfeed.js (the retired public-feed downloader), tests/backtest/data_loader.py `cross_source_drift`, OOS_VALIDATION.md, [[GAP-0001]], [[GAP-P12D-02]]

- Severity: low · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed (2026-09-11 — owner ratified in-chat HistData as the validation cross-check; master §13 → v2.13 and ACIS-09 → v1.2 name HistData, Dukascopy stays primary via its official S3 archive)

The spec's named validator cannot cross-check a 14-yr window. Found 2026-09-11 while sourcing data — external-referent class (the spec is wrong about the outside world).
- Justification: a source-name substitution in a list the master itself calls "examples, not a hardcode"; no guardrail or provenance rule changed.
- Proof: ACIS_SPEC.md §13 + CHANGELOG v2.13; specs/ACIS-09-BACKTEST_VALIDATION.md v1.2; `tests/backtest/sources.py` `HistDataTickSource`, `test_real_histdata_month_hour_is_sane`
- Residual: none — the cross-check source exists, is built and tested; running it before §14 is spec law (C-BT-9) scheduled in TODO.md.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-01]], tests/backtest/data_loader.py

## GAP-P12D-03: the §14 run needs three NON-tick inputs that are not yet sourced — SPX daily, a historical event calendar, time-varying financing
- Severity: high · Likelihood: high
- Status: open (must be sourced and wired before an honest §14 grade — tick data alone is NOT sufficient; owner ruling 2026-10-03, "After the cutover (Recommended)": the historical event calendar is built AFTER the 10-15..17 AWS move, with RSIM modelling the same macro gate so the demo and its replay stay matched, and PR16E-04 is decided then)
- Area: tests/backtest harness/metrics, core/data/normaliser.py `macro_imminent`, core/regime/nvma.py:268, master §14 / §3.5 / §9.5 / §13
- Description: (1) **SPX daily closes** — the §14 `spx_correlation` <0.25 gate needs a real, date-joined series ([[GAP-SP12E-06]]); today's call sites pass zeros. (2) **Historical high-impact event calendar** — `MarketStateFactory` takes an injected `macro_imminent(instrument, t)`. With no source it is always False, so NVMA never enters **MACRO_EVENT** (its top-precedence regime, nvma.py:268) and the Governor's §3.5 news blackout never fires: the backtest trades straight through NFP/FOMC/CPI where the live engine stands aside — optimistic AND unfaithful, and news-restricted rulebooks (ftmo, prop_demo) can never register a blackout breach. (3) **Financing** — the harness takes ONE constant `daily_swap` per instrument, defaulting to 0 "pending OANDA calibration"; 2012–2025 spans zero rates through the 2022-23 hiking cycle, so any constant misprices carry over most of the window and 0 flatters every overnight/weekend hold.
- **2026-10-03 (PR16E-04, [[GAP-P12D-150]]):** when the live calendar is wired, the 08:00 New York FX slot falls inside the macro gate's 30-minute lookahead (US releases 08:30 ET, ECB 08:15 NY), refusing target entries on 6 of 13 pairs on US release days with no retry: decide the slot or the gate's reach for target entries then.
- **2026-10-03 (GAP-P12D-159 PC-I-04; owner ruling, master v2.35 §3.6/§8.5):** when the news layer is wired, the Tier-G shock flag and the §8.5 tick-velocity anomaly must reach a daily target entry through the runtime's feed watch (`core/runtime/feed_check.py`), not NVMA. Today `wiring.py` passes `tierg_shock` and `macro_imminent` as False.
- Justification: (1) and (3) are small, free sources (daily index history; central-bank policy-rate histories as a rate-differential swap proxy, with the harness taking a dated series instead of a constant). (2) is the hard one — a curated historical high-impact set. Each is a declared evolve-driver input; until wired, a §14 report must carry these as caveats and must NOT be presented as a pass.
- Progress 2026-09-11: sources verified live (research) — SPX: Yahoo `^GSPC` primary, FRED `SP500` cross-check (≈10-yr window only; Stooq now needs a captcha key). Financing: OVERNIGHT rates, not policy rates (OANDA prices off tom-next ≈ overnight + ~1%/yr fee): DFF→SOFR, ECBDFR→€STR, SONIA, TONA (BoJ API), SARON (SNB), CORRA (BoC Valet), RBA cash rate; BIS WS_CBPOL only as fallback (JP hole 2013-04→2016-09; XM is the MRO rate until 2024-09, 40–50 bp above where euro overnight traded from 2015). Gold lease rates are unpublished since 2015 → declared sensitivity band. Calendar: primary-source Tier-1 build from official archives (BLS/Fed/ECB/BoE/BoJ/RBA/SNB/BoC/ONS…) with era-specific release times and IANA tz conversion; per-firm restricted lists + windows (FTMO ±2 min named list on funded accounts; FundedNext ±5 min; The5ers ±2 min opening only); Forex-Factory-derived dumps used for LOCAL cross-check only (ToS). The harness side of (3) is DONE ([[GAP-P12D-06]]: dated, direction-aware `financing` hook); the data + rate model remain.
- Progress 2026-09-11 (later): **(1) SPX and (3) financing DATA + MODEL DONE.** `scripts/download_macro.py` fetched 8/8 series 2011–2025 from the ISSUING institutions (FRED is unreachable from the owner's network — connects, never responds — so every rate comes from its central bank): SPX (Yahoo, 3,772 closes, validated against 5 well-known published closes), USD EFFR (NY Fed), EUR DFR (ECB), SONIA (BoE), TONA (BoJ), SARON (SNB, via OS TLS — its server omits an intermediate cert; verification stays on), CORRA (BoC), BIS policy rates ×7 (AUD source + cross-check). Raw responses + sha256 in `data/macro/manifest.json`. `tests/backtest/financing.py` builds the harness hook: direction × (r_base − r_quote) − fee (OANDA 1% majors), gold on a declared 0.25% lease assumption (0–1% sensitivity band), stale gaps >10 days raise. Real-data test reproduces ZIRP 2013, 2023 USD ≈5%, JPY/CHF negative eras, and positive 2023 USD_JPY long carry. **Remaining: (2) the historical event calendar, and wiring SPX (date-joined, [[GAP-SP12E-06]]) + financing into the evolve-driver.**
- Linked: tests/backtest/metrics.py `spx_correlation`, tests/backtest/harness.py `daily_swap`, core/data/normaliser.py `macro_imminent`, core/regime/nvma.py:268, [[GAP-SP12E-06]], config/platform_rules.yaml

## GAP-P12D-04: `cross_source_drift` matched ticks by linear scan — O(n·m), could never run on real data
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed 2026-09-11 (author-found while wiring real sources; regression test below)
- Area: tests/backtest/data_loader.py `_nearest` / `cross_source_drift`
- Description: every primary tick scanned the ENTIRE secondary list for its nearest match. Inert on 5-tick fixtures; on one real day of two ~50k-tick feeds it is ~2.5×10⁹ comparisons — the HistData↔Dukascopy check that [[GAP-P12D-01]]'s ratification is conditioned on could not have run.
- Justification: replaced by binary search over the sorted secondary; the only minimum-gap candidates are the latest tick at-or-before `ts` and the latest duplicate of the first tick after it, visited in time order with `<=`, so the old later-wins tie-break is reproduced exactly.
- Linked: test `test_bisect_nearest_matches_the_old_linear_scan_exactly` (3,000 probes × 4 tolerances on a duplicate-heavy grid vs the old scan), tests/backtest/test_sources.py
- Residual: none — the matcher is exact and fast; running it on real overlapping Dukascopy/HistData days is spec law (C-BT-9) scheduled in TODO.md's §14 data plan.

## GAP-P12D-05: backtest harness booked PnL in the QUOTE currency as if it were USD — USD_JPY inflated ~100–150× (CONFIRMED)
- Severity: high · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed 2026-09-11 (author-found while designing dated financing; regression tests below)
- Area: tests/backtest/harness.py `_close_at` / `_unrealised`
- Description: `gross = Δprice × units` is a QUOTE-currency amount; the harness added it straight into realised PnL, the marked equity, the intraday drawdown and every §14 metric. A ¥10,000 USD_JPY gain was booked as $10,000. USD_JPY is ENABLED, so any two-pair §14 run would have been dominated by yen-denominated noise — meaningless Sharpe/DD/PF. The live Risk Monitor was fixed for the same class at phase 9 ([[GAP-0104]]); the harness never was. The spec is silent on account currency, so the external referent governs: PnL is in account currency.
- Justification: every quote-currency amount (gross, friction, financing, marks) is converted once via `quote_to_account` — XXX_USD ×1, USD_XXX ÷price, crosses through the quote leg's last-seen price; a missing leg raises `ConversionUnavailable` (never books pounds as dollars). This also closes the backtest side of [[GAP-0092]]'s cross-pair residual (EUR_GBP is in the ratified universe; its GBP_USD leg is too).
- Linked: tests `test_usd_jpy_pnl_is_booked_in_dollars_not_yen`, `test_cross_pair_converts_through_its_quote_leg_or_refuses`, `test_usd_jpy_open_position_marked_in_dollars` (tests/backtest/test_harness_regressions.py)
- Residual: GAP-0092 — the backtest side is done; the LIVE Risk Monitor still falls back to quote currency for a cross pair, and that entry owns it.

## GAP-P12D-06: carry was one signed constant per instrument — direction- and size-blind — and billed weekends twice
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed 2026-09-11 (inert until now only because the constant defaulted to 0)
- Area: tests/backtest/harness.py `_carry_quote` / `_nights`, tests/backtest/carry.py
- Description: (a) `daily_swap[instrument]` was charged identically to a long and a short and did not scale with position size — real financing flips sign with direction and scales with notional (OANDA: position value × funding rate × 1/365). (b) `_nights` counted every 22:00-UTC rollover including Saturday and Sunday, while `swap_multiplier` also tripled Wednesday — the weekend was billed twice (+2 days per weekend held), contradicting carry.py's own tom-next rationale. (c) An undeclared `swap_convention` (e.g. XAU_USD's `financing_daily`) silently charged 1× every night.
- Justification: the harness now takes `financing(instrument, night, direction) -> annualised %` for THAT side (rate differential net of broker fee, built from the dated overnight-rate series — [[GAP-P12D-03]]) and charges value × rate/100/365 × multiplier per BUSINESS-day rollover. Conventions are explicit: tom_next = Wed ×3, financing_daily = Fri ×3 (OANDA CFD convention; not defined by the spec — declared here); anything else raises. Entry price stands in for the 5pm-ET rollover price (declared; holds ≤48 h).
- Linked: tests `test_financing_is_direction_and_size_aware`, `test_weekend_is_not_billed_twice`, `test_gold_financing_bills_the_weekend_on_friday`, `test_overnight_carry_reduces_pnl_of_a_held_position`, `test_triple_swap_wednesday_carry`
- Residual: GAP-P12D-03, GAP-P12D-07 — wiring the dated rate model into the evolve-driver (P12D-03) and the rollover INSTANT (P12D-07) are owned there.

## GAP-P12D-07: the spec's fixed 22:00-UTC daily anchor is the real FX rollover only in winter — in US summer time it is 21:00 UTC
- Severity: low · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed for the BACKTEST (2026-09-11 — OWNER RULING in-chat: "make sure that there is no time anomaly"; the engine side is GAP-P12D-10)
- Area: core/data/fx_clock.py (new), tests/backtest/harness.py financing nights + weekend-holding rule, tests/backtest/data_loader.py `_is_weekend`, master §C1 / §13
- Description: the FX day rolls at 17:00 New York; that is 22:00 UTC under EST but **21:00 UTC under EDT** (≈ Mar–Nov). The spec pins 22:00 UTC year-round. In summer that financed positions from the wrong rollover — the WRONG DAY, which changes whether the tom-next triple applies (a Tue-21:30 → Wed-21:30 hold paid Tuesday ×1 instead of Wednesday ×3) — dropped the real Sunday 21:00–22:00 UTC open, and placed the Friday close an hour late. Exactly the §13 DST-boundary glitch class.
- Justification: one dependency-free FX clock (`core/data/fx_clock.py`, exact US rule since 2007: 2nd Sunday of March → 1st Sunday of November; earlier dates raise) now defines the rollover, the weekend window and business-day rollovers; every backtest consumer uses it. No new dependency (the `tzdata` package would have needed a spec edit — A1).
- Proof: `test_daylight_time_switches_on_the_real_us_dates`, `test_rollover_is_21_utc_in_summer_and_22_utc_in_winter`, `test_summer_weekend_boundaries_follow_new_york`, `test_business_rollovers_are_weekdays_at_the_real_instant`, `test_summer_rollover_is_21_utc_not_22`
- Residual: GAP-P12D-10 — the ENGINE's fixed-UTC day boundaries were owned there (fixed the same day, master v2.14); what is left of that work is owned by GAP-P12D-11.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-06]], [[GAP-0023]]

## GAP-P12D-10: the engine's day boundaries and sessions were fixed-UTC — an hour off for half the year (owner ruling: no time anomaly)
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed 2026-09-11 (owner ruling "make sure that there is no time anomaly"; the guardrail-adjacent daily-loss instant explicitly owner-ratified in-chat 2026-09-11 — "I ratify all of them"; master v2.14)
- Area: config/calendars.py (new — local-time schema split out of config/schemas.py), config/system_config.yaml `session_boundaries`, config/instruments.yaml calendars, core/data/fx_clock.py (UK/Tokyo/Chicago zones, `local_to_utc`, `to_local`, `trading_day`, `next_local`), core/data/bars.py `DailyBarAggregator`, core/data/asset_classes.py, core/optimizer/param_library.py `is_session_boundary`, core/governor/capital_governor.py weekend ban + `_next_boundary`, core/risk/guardrails.py daily-loss day (GUARDRAIL-ADJACENT)
- Description: each read a fixed UTC clock time, and they disagreed with one another — the rollover/weekend/daily-loss day at 22:00 UTC (New York WINTER time) while London 07:00 and New York 12:00 were SUMMER times. So in summer the TSMOM daily bar closed an hour late, the weekend ban and the daily-loss reset fired an hour late, and in winter the London/New-York session tags and C1 staging instants sat an hour early.
- Justification: every market time is now declared as a LOCAL wall time `{zone, at}` (the schema has no UTC field left to misuse — the old keys are rejected) and resolved per date by the one dependency-free clock. The daily-loss limit no longer takes a reset parameter at all: its day is `fx_clock.trading_day` (17:00 New York). No limit VALUE, HALT/veto/resume semantics or staging rule changed — only when the already-ratified day and sessions begin. The GAP-0025/0033 cross-check compares `(zone, local time)` pairs. GAP-0023 (the uncovered New York close → Tokyo open window) was fixed separately the same day.
- Proof: `test_daily_loss_day_resets_at_the_real_new_york_rollover`, `test_daily_bars_anchor_at_the_new_york_rollover`, `test_windows_follow_each_centres_dst`, `test_boundaries_follow_dst`, `test_gap0053_weekend_ban_covers_the_whole_closed_window`, `test_boundary_in_the_wrong_zone_is_fatal`, `test_fixed_utc_calendar_fields_are_rejected`, `test_config_zones_are_exactly_the_modelled_zones`, `test_uk_summer_time_switches_on_the_real_dates`, `test_each_centre_opens_at_its_own_local_time_all_year`
- Residual: none — the follow-on gold-calendar shape (GAP-P12D-11) and the Sydney window (GAP-0023) were fixed the same day; the phase-12 dual audit re-examines this change as guardrail-adjacent (TODO item, brief point (d)).
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-07]], [[GAP-0023]], [[GAP-P12D-11]], core/data/fx_clock.py, master §3.7 / §C1

## GAP-P12D-11: XAU_USD (a ~23h/5 spot CFD) was session-tagged against a 6.5-hour CME day-session calendar
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed 2026-09-11 (owner directive in-chat "fix the other findings … leave nothing untouched"; master v2.15)
- Area: config/instruments.yaml `spot_metals` calendar + `XAU_USD.calendar`, config/calendars.py `FxCalendar.daily_break`, core/data/asset_classes.py, master §3.7 ("metals' daily break")
- Description: OANDA's XAU_USD trades roughly Sunday 18:00 → Friday 17:00 New York with a daily one-hour break, but it was declared on `cme_metals` (08:30–15:00 Chicago), so everything outside the US day session was `closed` ⇒ untradeable — ~17 h a day ignored, and §14 would have graded a gold strategy that could only trade the US day.
- Justification: spot metals are OTC, so they now use an fx-type calendar (`spot_metals`: the same centre sessions, week close Friday 17:00 New York, week open Sunday 18:00 New York) with a declared `daily_break` 17:00–18:00 New York. `cme_metals` stays declared for exchange-traded contracts (futures, GAP-0109).
- Proof: `test_trades_around_the_clock_except_the_daily_break`, `test_week_opens_an_hour_after_fx`, `test_gold_is_on_the_spot_metals_calendar`
- Residual: none — XAU_USD is declared-disabled; widening its tradeable hours is re-verified by the scenario-replay step the diversification-onboarding TODO already requires when it is enabled.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-10]], [[GAP-0109]], [[GAP-SP12E-01]]

## GAP-P12D-13: HistData timestamps follow EUROPEAN summer time, not the documented "EST without DST" — our reader is 1 h wrong for ~7 months of every year, and the drift check passes silently when nothing matches
- Severity: high · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed 2026-09-13 (owner go "go ahead"; master v2.16)
- Area: tests/backtest/sources.py `HistDataTickSource` (`EST_OFFSET` fixed +5 h), scripts/download_histdata.py `EST_FIXED` (month-boundary verify + manifest `first_utc`), tests/backtest/data_loader.py `cross_source_drift` (n_compared == 0 ⇒ `flagged=False`), tests/backtest/test_sources.py former `test_histdata_converts_fixed_est_to_utc` (encoded the vendor claim) + `test_real_histdata_month_hour_is_sane` (January only), master §13 v2.13 wording "fixed-EST timestamps", OOS_VALIDATION.md
- Description: HistData's spec page says "Eastern Standard Time (EST) time-zone WITHOUT Day Light Savings adjustments". Measured against Dukascopy (UTC; tick-for-tick identical to Dukascopy's own public feed), EUR/USD and USD/JPY HistData ticks match 100% on (millisecond, bid, ask) only after shifting by −1 h during EU summer time and 0 h otherwise, switching exactly on the EU dates: 2023-10-27 −1 h / 2023-10-30 0 h; 2024-03-29 0 h / 2024-04-01 −1 h; 2024-10-25 −1 h / 2024-10-28 0 h; 2024-03-11 and 2024-11-01 (US already/still on EDT) 0 h. I.e. HistData time = Frankfurt local time − 6 h. Consequences: (1) every HistData tick in EU summer time is placed 1 h late; (2) `cross_source_drift` aligns within 1 s, so a 1 h shift yields zero matches and a report with `n_compared=0, flagged=False` — a silent false CLEAR, exactly the failure the check exists to prevent; (3) the FX ticks are the SAME prices as Dukascopy (EUR/USD 2025-12: 1,293,972 ticks, max drift 0.0 bps; USD/JPY a filtered subset), so HistData is NOT an independent check of Dukascopy prices for FX — only of our decoding/timestamps (which it just did); XAU/USD differs in prices and timing (apparently independent).
- Justification: FIXED — (a) `tests/backtest/sources.py` converts HistData time as London wall time − 5 h via `core/data/fx_clock.py` (`histdata_naive_to_utc`, per-day offset cache; `utc_to_histdata` picks the monthly files), and the downloader's manifest times use the same function (still 400 lines); (b) `cross_source_drift` reports `coverage` and FLAGS below `min_coverage` (default 0.5) with a `reason` — zero overlap can no longer clear; (c) master §13 v2.16 + OOS_VALIDATION wording corrected; (d) the 560 manifest records written before the fix were corrected in place (383 EU-summer records; original kept as `data/histdata/manifest.jsonl.pre-p12d13`) with the downloader paused, then it was restarted on the fixed code. Independence of HistData for FX is a separate matter, owned by GAP-P12D-14.
- Proof: `test_histdata_clock_is_frankfurt_minus_six_hours`, `test_real_histdata_ticks_land_on_dukascopy_utc_across_eu_switches`, `test_cross_source_drift_flags_when_nothing_aligns`, `test_summer_month_first_utc_follows_eu_summer_time`, former `test_histdata_converts_fixed_est_to_utc`
- Residual: GAP-P12D-14 — HistData FX ticks are Dukascopy-derived, so an INDEPENDENT price check of Dukascopy (ECB rates, triangular, structural/time, gold) is still to build.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-14]], [[GAP-P12D-02]], [[GAP-P12D-04]], [[GAP-P12D-07]], tests/backtest/sources.py, tests/backtest/data_loader.py

## GAP-P12D-14: no INDEPENDENT validation of Dukascopy prices and timestamps exists yet — HistData's FX ticks are Dukascopy's own
- Severity: high · Likelihood: high
- Status: open (checks BUILT and tested 2026-09-13 — `scripts/download_ecb_rates.py`, `scripts/run_data_validation.py`, `tests/backtest/validation_*.py`; 2024 trial: 0 flags; the full 2012–2025 run was started 2026-09-13 and its report `data/validation/report.md` still needs review before this closes)
- Area: tests/backtest (new validation checks + runner), master §13 C-BT-9 wording, OOS_VALIDATION.md
- Description: GAP-P12D-13 showed HistData's EUR/USD and USD/JPY ticks carry Dukascopy's exact prices (EUR/USD 2025-12 tick-for-tick identical, 0.0 bps), so the §13 cross-source check against HistData cannot catch a Dukascopy PRICE error for FX — only decoding/clock errors. XAU/USD on HistData differs (apparently independent).
- Justification: the approved plan replaces "full Dukascopy-vs-HistData drift" for FX with independent, fast, free checks: (1) ECB daily euro reference rates vs Dukascopy mid at 14:15 Frankfurt — trial 2024 EUR/USD: 256 days, |diff| median 1.43 bps, p95 4.81, max 8.60, 0 days > 10 bps; (2) triangular consistency EUR/GBP ≈ EUR/USD ÷ GBP/USD at tick level; (3) structural/time checks from Dukascopy alone (17:00-New-York rollover spread spike, weekend boundaries, London/NY open tick-rate steps, NFP bursts); (4) gold via the (now clock-fixed) HistData XAU or OANDA practice M1 candles (owner-held token via env var). HistData stays downloading as an extra check (owner). Each check needs tests, a flag-never-average report, and the §13 C-BT-9 wording edit.
- Correction (2026-09-13): the sampled gold comparison returned 0.0 bps median AND worst on all 12 sample days of 2024 — HistData's gold is ALSO Dukascopy-derived there, so check 4 is not independent (the earlier "gold looks independent" rested on one 2023 night-time sample). An independent gold source (e.g. OANDA practice M1 candles, owner-held token) is still needed.
- Full run (2026-09-15, all 8 instruments, 2012–2025, 40,912 day files, 76 min; `data/validation/report.md`): **8 flags, each still to be explained.** (1) Gold vs HistData on 13 Mar 2013, 12 Mar 2014, 11 Mar 2015 and 14 Mar 2018 (medians of 6.9–21 bps). All four fall between the US and EU daylight-saving switches, so a one-hour clock offset in the sampled comparison is the first suspect, not a Dukascopy price error; from 2019 HistData gold matches Dukascopy at 0.0 bps. (2) AUD/USD: one day in 2016 (36.8 bps) and one in 2020 (164.9 bps) off the ECB reference; check those dates for a data spike versus a real market move. (3) USD/JPY 2012: 5,915 ticks inside the FX weekend window; all later years have 0. (4) XAU/USD 2025: the jobs-report burst at the true 08:30 New York (1.364) does not beat one hour late (1.379). Everything else passed: ECB medians 0.2–2.3 bps, the EUR/GBP triangle medians ≤ 0.2 bps, the rollover spread spike at the true 17:00 New York in every FX instrument-year, gold's daily break in every year.
- Proof: `test_ecb_fixing_is_1415_frankfurt_in_both_seasons`, `test_day_metrics_ecb_gap_and_weekend_ticks`, `test_a_clock_shifted_by_an_hour_is_flagged`, `test_triangle_flags_an_inconsistent_cross`, `test_a_price_disagreement_is_flagged`
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-13]], [[GAP-P12D-03]], [[GAP-SP12E-03]], tests/backtest/OOS_VALIDATION.md

## GAP-P12D-159: the pre-cutover dual audit (2026-10-03) — 20 findings (12 internal, 8 external), triaged by the lead
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (one item owed: PC-I-07's grader-built cost models checked against the claim's `cost_map_hash`)
- Area: the change `f0d9524..9f57a6f` (brief `archive/AUDIT_BRIEF_PRECUTOVER_2026-10-03.md`, pinned at `35fd443`; owner's go "Launch both now (Recommended)"); reports `archive/AUDIT_PRECUTOVER_INTERNAL_2026-10-03.md` (saved by the lead from the hand-back text; the harness refused the subagent's write) and `archive/AUDIT_PRECUTOVER_EXTERNAL_2026-10-03.md`
- Description: neither lens found a cutover blocker. The lead verified each finding before acting (a finder is not a clearer). The owner ruled the four decisions in chat on 2026-10-03, all "Recommended": the feed watch's floor and ceiling now; the spec batch (X-2, X-3, PC-I-04) as master v2.35; GOTOBI's two looks (the weekday contrast and stage C); the anchor's server-side gap recorded and decided with the compute home.
  - **PC-I-01 (medium), the feed watch never tested as wired: FIXED.** The runtime asserts `t.engine.feed_refusal == rt.feed.refusal` and that a fed tick ends `no_feed`; a loop test refuses the open at a slot and still sends the close. Proof: `test_a_feed_refusal_at_the_slot_stops_the_open_and_still_sends_the_close`, `_assert_the_target_wiring` in `test_a_runtime_from_the_shipped_config_wires_the_target_loop_and_silences_legacy` (the auditor's three mutants rerun on the server, recorded below).
  - **PC-I-02, the spread yardstick: FIXED (master v2.35, ACIS-05 v1.12).** CONFIRMED on the demo's own log before the ruling: EUR_USD quotes 0.0 on 83.5% of ticks. On those ticks v2.33 refused 11.7% of moments at random; v2.35 refuses 0.8%; USD/JPY is unchanged at 1.9%. The rule is now 3 × max(recent median, one price tick), plus the normaliser's `implausible_spread` flag as an absolute ceiling. Proof: `test_a_raw_spread_feed_whose_median_is_zero_is_judged_against_one_price_tick`, `test_a_spread_the_normaliser_flags_implausible_is_refused_however_slowly_it_widened`.
  - **PC-I-03, the shadow book's convention: FIXED.** `demo_report.py` declares the close-to-close signal mark and that it is not RSIM's fill-to-fill book: stage D grades a replay of the live rule, never this report. A day decided only at boot now counts, once. Proof: `test_a_day_decided_only_at_boot_still_marks_and_a_re_derivation_does_not_double_count`.
  - **PC-I-04, Tier-G and §8.5 routed through NVMA: RULED (master v2.35 §3.6/§8.5).** Both reach a target entry through the feed watch when the news layer is wired. The build is owned by [[GAP-P12D-03]].
  - **PC-I-05, the in-flight docstrings: FIXED.** `portfolio.py` and `executor.py` now say opens AND closes are in flight. DECLARED: a close the venue then rejects counts as filled until its report, so a later entry in the same slot may be judged against exposure that never materialised. It errs on the side of tighter caps for that slot's entries only.
  - **PC-I-06 (medium) and PCE-01 (medium), the anchor: FIXED in code; the branch-protection gap RECORDED.** `anchored()` fetches the remote's branch with a forced refspec, then requires every anchored line, in strictly rising order, to name the register's prefix head, ending at the current head. The real register passes (20 lines). Proof: `test_a_tail_rolled_back_and_rewritten_is_not_anchored_though_its_new_head_was_pushed`, `test_a_forged_local_ref_cannot_stand_in_for_the_remote`. The private repo on a free plan cannot protect the branch, so a force-push by a push-rights holder still goes undetected; the owner ruled "Record it; decide later": [[GAP-P12D-86]].

The FX slot (08:00 New York) is outside the hold, but the 17:00 decision is inside it, so netting belongs at the decision. Otherwise D1 skips every gotobi day with `position_changed`. G-3 conflicts with D-a's one-lot cap. The swap is now noted.

  - **X-1, GOTOBI's looks label their benchmark "PASSIVE_LONG" but store the control: ACCEPTED.** The register is append-only and the declaration has only that name. The label is recorded in [[GAP-P12D-158]], and a verdict never reads it.
  - **X-2 and X-3: FIXED (master v2.35, ACIS-09 v1.13).**
  - **THE v2 LOOKS (2026-10-03, `--line v2` at `04d64d7`; both lines registered before either grade; looks `000021` (stage C) and `000022` (contrast), outcomes `000023`/`000024`, anchored; register and `gotobi_v2.json` copied to `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/validation/2026-10-03g/`):**

This matches the one outside post-2023 measurement (PCE-02's blog, UNCONFIRMED). Survival by the rule credits nothing, and the forward premise the live draft leans on is now weak.

The demo logs the sleeve's would-be trades each gotobi day (a shadow record), and GOTOBI is revisited if that record turns positive (build owned by [[GAP-P12D-158]]). "Yes, before its build": REBAL v1a gets its own stage C on the 2024–25 holdout, once, kill-only (`scripts/r1_rebal_kill_test.py --stage-c`; P&L-blind dry check: 502 sessions, all priced).
  - **REBAL v1a's STAGE C (2026-10-03, `--stage-c` at `4dddff5`; look `000025`, outcome `000026`, anchored; copied to `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/validation/2026-10-03g/`): net Sharpe −0.17, HAC t −0.27, NOT killed**, −0.21 at 2× cost; the passive long US500 earned 1.25. Context from the stored vector: 2024 +0.06, 2025 −0.33. Like GOTOBI it survives the kill rule but shows no edge on the holdout. The position is short-leaning (mean −0.18) in a strong two-year rally. The script change (`--stage-c`) is not covered by a test, as PC-I-09 accepted for REBAL's script; the holdout is now spent for both.

It was −0.08 at 2× cost; the passive long earned 0.97. Context from the stored vector: 2024 −0.53, 2025 +0.55.

- Justification: open for PC-I-07's owed cost-map check (no look differs today: the scripts pass `cost_model(cfg, …)` from the config the register hashed). Every other finding is fixed, mitigated to a named owner, or accepted above.

## GAP-P12D-158: how the next mechanisms are chosen — the owner's ranking rule with the evidence fix, and the candidate pipeline
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (standing rule; owner ruling 2026-10-03 "With the evidence fix (Recommended)", on the owner's note "build on others' work, ranked by forced flows")
- Area: the edge slate (`archive/RESEARCH_EDGE_SHARPEN_2026-10-01.md`), every future kill-only look
- Description: **The rule.** Rank a candidate first by a reason its return PERSISTS — a forced or constrained counterparty (rebalancing, hedging, fixing flows, index changes), then a diversified risk premium (trend, carry, value), last a pure calendar or pattern effect — AND by evidence that it survived its publication or a market-structure change; only then by headline Sharpe, discounted about half for publication.
  - **Why the evidence fix (checked 2026-10-03):** forced flows decay too. The S&P 500 inclusion effect fell from 7.4% in the 1990s to 0.3% in the last decade (Greenwood & Sammon, JF 2025); the 4 pm WM/Reuters fix reversal disappeared after 2015, when the window widened (FCA Occasional Paper 46, 2018); across 97 anomalies returns are 26% lower out of sample and 58% lower after publication (McLean & Pontiff, JF 2016). D3's flat result fits.
  - **Breadth by mechanism, not instruments:** the target is two or three weak but independent edges; each candidate is judged by its expected correlation with D1 and REBAL.
  - **Never copy-trade:** no signal services, purchased EAs or crowded retail setups (survivorship; FTMO forbids third parties trading an account, and E8 flags herds running one purchased EA).
  - **Process unchanged:** an LLM-selected paper is kill-only on history and earns credit only forward (§5.6); one kill-only look at a time; related questions to the owner as one ask.
- **The pipeline (after REBAL, in order; the lead judges each before building):**
  1. **IMOM**, intraday momentum into the index cash close: Baltussen, Da, Lammers & Martens, JFE 2021 (60+ futures, 1974–2020); forced trader = gamma hedgers (option dealers, leveraged ETFs); needs 1-minute index candles and the venue's index contract size (the research's binding cost unknown); every index venue; expected correlation about 0 with D1, and REBAL is daily and contrarian.
  2. **GOTOBI**, USD/JPY into the 09:55 Tokyo fix on gotobi days: Bessho, Sugimoto & Suzuki 2023 (2018–2020 only); forced trader = importers settling at the fix; needs minute USD/JPY candles around 09:55 and a full Japanese business-day calendar; every FX venue.
  3. **H5**, the month-end London 4 pm fix flow: PARKED (FCA OP46: the fix reversal is gone after 2015; the research's practitioner test was marginal).
- **IMOM progress 2026-10-03 (owner: "IMOM research (Recommended)" until the cutover):** the paper read (Baltussen et al. 2021, eq. 2 and 12: hold sign(previous close -> 30 min before the close) over the last half hour; equity futures 1/N, 1974-2020, Sharpe 1.73 gross against 1.07 for Gao's first-half-hour predictor; no costs in the paper, a positive net Sharpe for S&P futures at one tick). BUILT: the 1-minute BID/ASK candles of the six explored indices, 2012-2025 (59,358 files; synced on the AWS data server, one archive sha256 `96d62c3b...` pulled and matched; `scripts/download_dukascopy_minutes.py`, `tests/backtest/candles_min.py`, a vectorised decoder), `tests/backtest/event_intraday.py` (prices at local wall times from the last quoted minute, the last-half-hour book), `scripts/r1_imom_kill_test.py` with a P&L-blind `--dry-check`. All 59,358 files verified (29,679 day pairs, 0 failures; `data/r1_real/dukascopy_minutes/manifest.jsonl`; the archive stays in the AWS transfer bucket). **Dry check (P&L-blind):** 2,877 of 2,998 NYSE sessions priced (121 without a 15:30 quote: about 36 half days, the rest feed holes); the quoted spread at 15:30 and 16:00 is 1.5 bp median (3.2 bp p95), so a round trip costs about 2.1 bp with C8 slippage, against the paper's ~2.7 bp gross per trade before decay. **Owner rulings 2026-10-03 (all "Recommended"):** the rest-of-day predictor (Baltussen); v1a trades every session and v1b only sessions whose |ROD| reaches the top third of the trailing 250 sessions (causal; `event_intraday.simulate_imom(top=2/3)`), BOTH registered before either is graded, forward eligibility pre-declared as survival plus a cost check on the demo's own spreads; US500 only.
- **THE LOOKS (2026-10-03, `scripts/r1_imom_kill_test.py` at `7a38ba1`; both lines registered before either grade; looks `000011` (v1a) and `000012` (v1b), outcomes `000013`/`000014`, anchored on GitHub `look-anchor`; register and `imom.json` copied to `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/validation/2026-10-03e/`):** graded 2012-02-01 to 2023-12-29 (3,108 marks; 161 sessions skipped).
  - **v1a (every session, 2,837 traded): net annual Sharpe −0.715, HAC t −2.55: KILLED**; −1.98 at 2× cost.
  - **v1b (top-third |ROD|, 909 traded): −0.219, HAC t −0.77: KILLED**; −0.74 at 2× cost.
  - Context: the passive long over the same half hour earned −0.26.
  - **Context, post-hoc, never a selection:** interpolating linearly between the 1× and 2× results puts gross at roughly +0.55 (v1a) and +0.30 (v1b). The dry check predicted this: a ~2.1 bp CFD round trip against the paper's ~2.7 bp gross per trade. The CFD's spread, not the signal, is what kills it, and a futures venue at one tick (~0.6 bp) is outside the swap-free CFD scope.
  - Killed by the pre-registered rule, so IMOM leaves the pipeline; next in the owner's order is GOTOBI.
- **GOTOBI progress 2026-10-03 (third session).**
  - **The lead's judgement: build.** The counterparty is constrained: importers settle at the 09:55 TTM fix. The effect survived Ito & Yamada's 2016 working paper into Bessho et al.'s 2018–2020 sample. The evidence is thin: a four-page note with profit factors and no t-statistics (Bessho H1, 03:00→09:55 JST on Tue–Fri gotobi days: N 185, PF 1.46 net of spread; non-gotobi control PF 0.51).
  - **Definitions differ.** Bessho: days 5/10/15/20/25/30, a holiday moved to the previous bank day, Tue–Fri only. Ito & Yamada: days 5/10/15/20/25/30 (their "5th and 10th days" is shorthand) AND the month's last trading day (corrected 2026-10-03, PCE-04).
  - **BUILT:**
    - `scripts/download_jp_holidays.py`: the Cabinet Office's own CSV, 1,067 holidays 1955–2027, in `data/r1_real/jp_holidays`.
    - `tests/backtest/jp_days.py`: bank days and gotobi days. It matches `exchange_days`' vetted XTKS month-turn rules on every in-scope day 2012–2024, except the TSE's 2020-10-01 outage, a day the banks were open.
    - `bi5.decode_arrays`: vectorised; it equals the scalar decoder.
    - `tests/backtest/event_window.py`: prices from the last tick at or before each JST wall time, legs and gated legs, P&L on the 17:00 New York mark.
    - `scripts/r1_gotobi_kill_test.py`, with `--dry-check`.
  - **Dry check (P&L-blind, 2012–2023):**
    - Tue–Fri gotobi days: 745 under the "30" rule, 748 under month-end; about 62 a year. 115 Monday gotobi days are excluded, and 1,649 control days remain.
    - All 740 graded days have prices.
    - Median spreads are 0.34 bp at 03:00, 0.40 bp at 09:00, 0.36 bp at 09:55 and 0.36 bp at 12:00 JST, against 1.15 bp at 07:00 (the rollover) and 0.66 bp at 08:00. The research's post-rollover entry (X3) would pay about three times Bessho's 03:00 spread, and the swap-free venue makes the rollover free to hold through.
  - **Owner rulings 2026-10-03 (third session, all "Recommended"):** entry at 03:00 JST (Bessho's own time); Bessho's day rule (5/10/15/20/25/30, the month's last day when it is shorter, moved to the previous bank day, Tue–Fri); register BOTH v1a (H1) and v1b (H1+H2) before either grade, each kill-only, with forward eligibility pre-declared as survival plus a cost check on the demo's own spreads; push.
  - **THE LOOKS (2026-10-03, third session; `scripts/r1_gotobi_kill_test.py` at `63d85f5`; both lines registered before either grade; looks `000015` (v1a) and `000016` (v1b), outcomes `000017`/`000018`, anchored on GitHub `look-anchor`; register, `gotobi.json` and the holiday calendar copied to `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/validation/2026-10-03f/`):** graded 2012-02-01 to 2023-12-29 (3,108 marks; 740 days traded, none skipped).
    - **v1a (H1): net annual Sharpe 1.81, HAC t 6.51, NOT killed**; 1.54 at 2× cost.
    - **v1b (H1+H2): 1.96, HAC t 7.12, NOT killed**; 1.61 at 2× cost.
    - The control (the same legs on the 1,649 non-gotobi Tue–Fri bank days) earned −0.07 and +0.08.
    - Survival credits nothing: the family is LLM-selected, so it is kill-only and earns credit only forward (§5.6).
  - **Context, post-hoc, from the stored vectors only (no new variant was simulated):**
    - v1a per trade: mean +5.3 bp, median +4.8 bp, sd 22 bp, hit rate 64%.
    - Positive in 11 of 12 years, and 1.61 without its 10 best days.
    - Decaying: 2.21 in 2012–17, 1.30 in 2018–23, and −0.51 in 2023.
    - Correlation with REBAL v1a 0.03, and with D1-FX13 −0.01.
    - The clock was spot-checked against the scalar decoder (2019-05-15, 2015-07-10: the entry tick at 17:59:5x UTC and the exit tick at 00:54:59 UTC, the same prices).
  - **What the book idealises (fix before any forward trade):**
    - It exits at the last tick at or before 09:55:00, the fix itself, at Dukascopy's quote. A live exit must fire seconds earlier on the venue's own quote, into the fix-time flow.
    - It assumes Dukascopy's spread, never the demo's.
    - Exit-time sensitivity (09:50, 09:45) would be a NEW look: register it first, never simulate it ad hoc.
  - **The pre-cutover audit on GOTOBI (2026-10-03, [[GAP-P12D-159]]):**
    - Statistics, clock and prices sound: the HAC t is 5.7–6.7 at every lag 0–100, the tick files are UTC days, and prices match the scalar decoder tick for tick.

Signal: each country's total-market return from the close of the month's first day to the close of its second-to-last. The currency trades 15:00→16:00 London on the last day; pooled demeaned slope −0.0142 (10% outperformance → 14 bp), R² 0.03. 72% of the move reverts by noon the next day (the fade H5 tested and FCA OP46 found gone after 2015).
  - **BUILT:** `tests/backtest/fxhedge.py` (the fix day, the per-exchange signal, the weights, the book, the cost gate, the reform split) and `scripts/r1_fxhedge_kill_test.py` with `--dry-check`. Five countries (US500, EU50, UK100, JP225, AUS200 against USD, EUR, GBP, JPY, AUD), four tick pairs; every departure declared in the script's header.

Five fix days fall on UK bank holidays (WM fixes on them; declared).

    - The predicted move per currency-month is a median 1.7–2.3 bp, against a C8 round trip of 0.6–0.95 bp (AUD 1.9 bp: it clears its cost in only 47% of months). Cost takes 22% of the expected gross.

- **GOLD-EW (2026-10-03, fourth session; owner: "GOLD-EW (Recommended)").**
  - **The source, read in full** (Donati & Jung, "Gold Price Dynamics Around the Clock", CBS master's thesis, 2019): COMEX GC futures, 5-minute mids, 2001-01..2018-12, New York time. Gold rises 18:00→02:30 NY (Tokyo and Shanghai) and falls through London's hours to about 10:00 NY. The thesis's rules: **Long 11:00→02:00 NY, Short 02:00→11:00 NY, Combo = both**. Combo's gross Sharpe was 1.61 over 2001–2018, but its gross return was 22.4%/yr over 2013–2018, only 5.1% in 2017 and −4.3% in 2018. Net of 100% of a 3 bp spread, Combo made −13.1% in 2017 and −27.2% in 2018. The thesis's own sample shows the decay.
  - **Ex ante, P&L-blind (Dukascopy XAU_USD hourly candles, 2012-02..2023-12, 3,108 weekdays, ~33 without a quoted hour at either time):** the median spread is 2.15 bp at 02:00 NY and 1.97 bp at 11:00 NY. One C8 round trip costs about 3.7 bp, so **Combo pays about 18.8%/yr and Long-only about 9.4%/yr.** Against the thesis's 2013–2018 gross (Combo 22.4%, Long 8.9%), the expected net is **about +3.6%/yr for Combo (a Sharpe of ~0.25) and about 0 for Long, before any decay**. After 2016 the thesis's own gross was at or below zero.
  - **The side agent's finding (owner: "fix that"): the evidence that selected GOLD-EW overlaps the windows meant to judge it.** The thesis covers 2001–2018. The WGC attribution covers 2024, and the desk notes cover 2025 H1. **The window roles are FIXED NOW, before any look** (owner-directed):
    - **2012–2018** overlaps the thesis's sample: kill-only, no evidence for the effect.
    - **2019–2023** is the only clean history (after the thesis, before the 2024–25 notes). Its net Sharpe is reported as a pre-declared segment, and if it is ≤ 0 the lead recommends DROP whatever the full-window verdict.
    - **2024–25 (stage C)** was seen by the selecting evidence (WGC 2024, desk notes 2025). It may still KILL, but a pass there is declared NON-EVIDENCE.
    - **Credit comes only from forward data** after the selection date (§5.6; the claim's certifying boundary is 2026-10-03).
  - **Owner ruling (2026-10-03, fourth session): "Drop it; OPEX next (Recommended)". GOLD-EW is DROPPED on ex-ante costs, no look spent** (no register line). Like IMOM, the CFD's round trip eats a published gross that had already decayed in its own sample. A revival needs a venue with about 1 bp gold round trips, or a version trading once a week or less: a new family, registered first.
- **OPEX (2026-10-03, fourth session; owner: "Drop it; OPEX next (Recommended)"): IN PROGRESS.** The source is read before anything is built. The mechanism is option market makers' hedge rebalancing. The week after shows some reversal.

The contrast's short leg is 0.306; 6 sessions have no anchor. The median US500 spread at the anchor is 1.31 bp (p95 3.28). Costs are about 3 bp a month, negligible. **Ex ante, the long-only book is mostly the equity premium; the contrast is the anomaly's test, and at the paper's excess (~0.3%/week before decay) it is worth about 0.2–0.3 Sharpe.**

The practitioner backtests' "+0.2% a week" that selected OPEX was the equity premium. v1a's survival is beta at about a quarter of the time, below the passive long, and is not forwarded.

## GAP-P12D-157: REBAL (institutional rebalancing pressure on index CFDs) is the next mechanism to kill-test on history
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (2026-10-03, owner ruling "REBAL (Recommended)", after D3 was dropped)
- Area: to build: a US 10-year bond return series (FRED DGS10 with a duration approximation, P&L-blind), the 60/40 drift signal (threshold and month-end calendar rebalancing), the grader (RSIM's daily clock or the event simulator), a kill-test script; source `archive/RESEARCH_EDGE_SHARPEN_2026-10-01.md` N1
- Description: Harvey, Mazzoleni & Melone (version of 2026-01-14, SSRN 5122748): about $20 trillion of 60/40-style money rebalances on thresholds and at month-end, predictably from public prices; "when stocks are overweight, funds sell stocks and buy bonds, leading to a decrease in equity returns of 17 basis points over the next day". Their strategy earns Sharpe 1.11 over 1997-09 to 2023-03 (net "close to 1"). It is mostly the SHORT side (which a swap-free account does not penalise either) and contrarian to TREND. It was SELECTED by the 2026-10-01 research agents (an LLM), so it is kill-only on 2012–2023 (t < −0.5), like D1 and D3. Declared deviations owed: the equity leg only (no bond CFDs), the US 60/40 signal driving the non-US indices.
- **The paper, read 2026-10-03 (NBER w33554 / SSRN 5122748, the 2026-01-14 version, Appendix B and §4):** two simulated 60/40 portfolios on daily E-mini S&P 500 and 10-year note futures returns. THRESHOLD resets to 60/40 the day after its equity weight drifts ≥ δ, CALENDAR after each month's last business day; each signal is the drifted equity weight − 60% at the close. The Threshold signal averages δ = 0%, 0.1%, …, 2.5%. The position is the mean of −Threshold/1.5% and the Calendar term: sign(−Calendar) in the month's last 5 business days, sign(Calendar at day −4) on the first business day of the next month, 0 otherwise. It is applied to S&P minus the note, close to close. Next-day pressure per 1σ of signal: equities −16 bp (Threshold) and −17 bp (Calendar); bonds +4 bp and +2 bp. So most of the timing is in the equity leg, and the bond leg mostly hedges a strategy that leans short. The international evidence is only a lagged-signal regression.
- **Owner rulings 2026-10-03 (all "Recommended"):**
  - (1) v1 trades the US500 equity leg with the paper's signal and position, close to close at the US cash close.
  - (2) "Register both": v1a (that equity leg) and v1b (the paper's full S&P-minus-note strategy) are BOTH registered before either is graded, each kill-only (HAC t < −0.5). PRE-DECLARED: forward eligibility is decided by survival plus venue fit, never by which earned more.
  - (3) The owner checks the 10-year T-Note's symbol on the demo account's cTrader app; v1b is forwardable only there.
  - **Correction:** the lead's earlier premise "bonds aren't tradable at our venues" was wrong. Pepperstone (the demo venue) lists a US T-Note 10yr bond-futures CFD on all its platforms, cTrader included (its site; not yet verified on our account). FTMO lists none, and E8's CFD list shows none.
- **BUILT 2026-10-03:** `scripts/download_treasury.py` (the US Treasury's daily par yields 2011–2025, from the issuer; 3,750 days, the documented 0.54 record low reproduced; `data/r1_real/treasury`), `tests/backtest/rebal.py` (the paper's equations), `tests/backtest/event_daily.py` (the close-to-close book; quoted hours per [[GAP-P12D-156]]), `evaluate_events` split into `register_event_look` / `grade_event_look` (both lines before either grade), and `scripts/r1_rebal_kill_test.py`.
- **Dry check (P&L-blind, 2012-01..2023-12, 3,018 sessions), against the paper's stated facts:** the Threshold–Calendar correlation is 0.61 (paper ≈ 0.60); the 1.1% band resets 11.3 times a year (≈ monthly) and the 2.5% band 2.5 times (paper ≈ quarterly; 2012–2023 was calmer than 1997–2023); both signals have positive means; the Calendar term is live on 29% of days; the position averages −0.13 (short-leaning, as the paper says), |position| 0.28, daily turnover 0.16. Six graded sessions have no quoted US500 hour.
- Proof: `test_the_papers_worked_example_a_10pct_equity_day_moves_the_weight_by_2_26pct`, `test_threshold_resets_the_day_after_a_breach_and_averages_26_bands`, `test_calendar_resets_the_day_after_the_months_last_business_day`, `test_the_position_is_the_mean_of_the_scaled_threshold_and_the_week4_and_reversal_signs`, `test_the_note_leg_moves_by_the_7_year_par_duration_and_a_missing_yield_repeats`, `test_each_session_earns_the_position_set_at_the_previous_close_and_pays_on_the_trade`, `test_a_session_without_an_anchor_neither_marks_nor_trades_and_the_next_books_its_move`, `test_the_spread_version_is_short_the_compounded_note_between_anchors`, `test_a_moving_quote_with_zero_volume_counts_as_a_price`
- **THE LOOKS (2026-10-03, `scripts/r1_rebal_kill_test.py` at `2bbdc27`; both lines registered before either grade; looks `000007` (v1a) and `000008` (v1b), outcomes `000009`/`000010`, anchored on GitHub `look-anchor`; register, outputs and the Treasury inputs copied to `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/validation/2026-10-03d/`):** graded 2012-02-01 to 2023-12-29 (3,108 marks, 6 sessions without a quoted price).
  - **v1a (US500 alone): net annual Sharpe 0.495, HAC t 1.82, NOT killed**; 0.43 at 2× cost.
  - **v1b (US500 minus the note): 0.388, HAC t 1.39, NOT killed**; 0.23 at 2× cost.
  - Context: the passive long US500 earned 0.71. Survival credits nothing; both are kill-only.
  - **Context, post-hoc, never a selection:** v1a's correlation with D1-FX13's daily series is −0.005, with the passive long 0.23, and with v1b 0.97. Its Sharpe was 0.17 in 2012–17 and 0.69 in 2018–23 (the paper says the effect grows with 60/40 assets), and 0.42 without 2020-02-20..04-30, so it is not one crisis. An equal-risk D1 + v1a mix would have earned 0.47. The note leg cost v1b money over 2012–2023, against the side agent's expectation.
  - **Pre-declared forward rule (survival plus venue fit):** both survived. v1a trades wherever US500 does (every venue); v1b needs a 10-year note CFD (Pepperstone lists one, our account unverified; FTMO and E8's CFDs list none), and at 0.97 correlation it adds little beside v1a.
  - **Declared idealization to fix before any forward trade:** the paper and this book compute the signal from the close at which they trade (the S&P's 16:00 close; the Treasury's par yields use about 15:30 quotes). A live version must decide a few minutes before the close from live prices, and fill by the close.
  - **Provenance note:** the looks recorded `ust_par.csv` as sha256 `cb3e5570…` (the CRLF file Windows wrote); the manifest's `b0dd60e1…` hashed the same text with LF endings (verified equal after the CRLF→LF swap). The downloader now writes bytes, so the manifest hashes what is on disk.
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-03, "Spec now, build after cutover (Recommended)":** v1a's live path is drafted in `archive/REBAL_LIVE_DRAFT_2026-10-03.md` (rulings L-1..L-6: a 15:50 New York decision filled by 15:58, the CFD mid as the equity input, a one-session-lagged Treasury 7-year yield, one-lot sizing on `capital_ref` under every guard, weekend holding, stage D on the live rule), ratified and built AFTER the AWS move, with its own audit; v1b is not forwarded. Before the build, the live timing gets its own kill-only look on 1-minute candles (IMOM's download).
- Justification: open: the live build waits for the cutover; TODO START HERE carries it.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-154]], [[GAP-P12D-148]], [[GAP-P12D-27]], [[GAP-P12D-156]]

## GAP-P12D-156: the candle store counts an hour as a price only when Dukascopy reports volume, and Dukascopy reports zero volume on moving index quotes for months at a time
- Severity: low · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (2026-10-03, found by D3's P&L-blind dry check)
- Area: tests/backtest/candle_store.py (`traded` = volume > 0), read by tests/backtest/rsim_real.py (RSIM) and tests/backtest/event_sim.py
- Description: measured on the 2012–2023 hourly candles, months in which more than 20% of the hours whose price moved read volume 0: US500 2012-01, 2012-09, 2013-01..05 and 2013-10 (in 2013-03 and 2013-04, every moving hour); UK100 2013-03..05; JP225 2013-03..05; none for US30, EU50 or AUS200. Those hours are real quotes the store discards, so RSIM's closes go stale over them (past 4 days: no close) and D3 skips the affected month turns. Separately, JP225 and AUS200 quoted nothing during their Asian cash sessions from 2015-02 to 2015-11 (flat, volume 0: a frozen feed, rightly not a price), as did UK100 on 2014-12-30. FX and metals were not scanned.
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-03, "Count moving quotes (Recommended)":** the event simulator's daily book (`event_daily.quoted`) counts an hour as a price when it traded OR its price moved; a frozen flat hour stays excluded. On US500 2012-02..2023-12, sessions without a price fall from 88 to 6. RSIM's rule is unchanged until this entry's decision and its parity check.
- **2026-10-03 (GAP-P12D-159 PC-I-10):** the event books use three "quoted" rules: REBAL counts a moved mid, IMOM a moved bid or a trade, and D3 a trade. Unify them as one shared predicate together with this entry's decision, before the next look on those books.
- Justification: open. Outcome-neutral for the looks made: a hole is unrelated to returns, so a skipped turn or a stale close shrinks the sample without biasing it. D1's 21-instrument look ([[GAP-P12D-151]]) read US500, UK100 and JP225 through it. Owed: scan FX and metals; decide whether "moved with volume 0" counts as a price (a change to RSIM's observation rule, with its own parity check) before the next look that leans on 2012–2013 index data.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-154]], [[GAP-P12D-151]], [[GAP-P12D-136]]

## GAP-P12D-155: the Claude Code platform updates (2026-08-21 → 10-02) — stale model pins, the launch-rule contradiction, auditor role files, and the read-path hygiene owed since 2026-07-21
- Severity: low · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (2026-10-03; owner rulings in chat 2026-10-03, all "Recommended": "Re-pin to Opus 5.5", "Reword :76 to defer to 09-25", "Create them now", "Shrink it to a pointer")
- Area: CLAUDE.md (the line-3 pin, the adoption-gate pointer, the audit section), STATUS.md, the two auditor role files under `.claude/agents/`, `archive/CONSTITUTION_ADOPTION_GATE_2026-07-21.md`; the read path
- Description: The owner had Anthropic's "This week in Claude Code" recommendations applied across the project family. Two model launches (Fable 5.1, Opus 5.5) had left ACIS's read path naming superseded models: the pin read "model floor Opus 4.8; audits run on Opus 5", with no literal `model-in-charge` (A2). The audit section ordered a session to "launch two background Opus 4.8 subagents" at every phase end, contradicting the 2026-09-25 directive that only the owner launches agents. The 2026-07-21 adoption gate said "FIRST ACTION … before the TODO.md worklist", contradicting "Start every session from TODO.md", while its step 2 sat in prose, unledgered.
  **Applied 2026-10-03 (owner rulings):**
  - model-in-charge and floor Opus 5.5, and audits on Opus 5.5 (CLAUDE.md, STATUS.md, the auditor memory);
  - the audit section now asks first, then launches the two lenses from the role files `acis-auditor-internal` and `acis-auditor-external` (opus, effort xhigh, no memory; the internal lens has no web, and the external lens's search cap is 15 unless the brief sets one);
  - the gate block moved verbatim to the archive, with a one-line pointer left in place;
  - TODO's header version corrected to v2.33.

  **Owed:**
  - (1) Run `/doctor prompt-audit` at a session start and triage its findings under CLAUDE.md's NEVER list: rules, specs and tests stay owner-ruled.
  - (2) The gate's step 2, documentation hygiene. The read path is not short: the CLAUDE.md status line is one paragraph of about 1,900 words, and the audit-model paragraph about 430.
- Justification: open. The owed items are read-path work for a quiet slot, never ahead of the cutover (TODO's sort key). The role files are definitions only: every launch still needs the owner's explicit go (2026-09-25).
- Linked: [[GAP-C10-01]], [[GAP-C09-01]], [[GAP-P12M-02]]

## GAP-P12D-154: D3 (the turn-of-month index long) is the next mechanism to kill-test on history; its faithful event harness is half-built
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: closed (2026-10-03, owner ruling "Drop it (Recommended)": D3 earned nothing on history and is dropped; earlier rulings "Faithful event harness (Recommended)" and "Papers' window (Recommended)", master v2.34; built and looked at the same day)
- Area: tests/backtest/exchange_days.py, event_sim.py, evaluate_events.py; `look_claims.SIMULATORS`/`OPEN_SIMULATORS` gain "event"; scripts/r1_d3_kill_test.py (the branch `wip/d3-event-harness` is superseded, not merged)
- Description: D3 = R1-B's slate item (McConnell & Xu 2008; Etula et al. 2020): long from 14:30 New York on a month's last trading day (T−1) to the home cash close of the next month's third (T+3), zero free parameters, on US500, US30, UK100, JP225, EU50, AUS200. Like D1 it is literature SELECTED by the cloud lead's plan, so kill-only on history (t < −0.5). RSIM's daily clock cannot express its timing, so the owner chose a faithful event simulator, widening the open scope from "rsim" to "rsim + event". The branch holds `simulate()` (trading days from each index's cash session, the windows, entry and exit fills, 17:00-New-York daily marks, equal-risk legs on trailing 60-mark vol, real spreads plus the declared slippage, swap-free). Owed: (1) `evaluate_events` mirroring `evaluate()`: register first, `begin_grade(simulator="event")`, refuse any event look that is not kill-only (no event verdict exists for a pass), chain the outcome with the daily vector; (2) add "event" to `SIMULATORS` and `OPEN_SIMULATORS` and update the scope test; (3) unit tests on a synthetic Hourly (windows, the no-look-ahead vol, cost booking, a planted turn-of-month effect earning and a null not); (4) the script, a P&L-blind dry check, a clean commit, then the one look. If D3 survives, the shelved index onboarding ([[GAP-P12D-27]]) returns.
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-03 (second session), "Papers' window (Recommended)":** D3 v1 holds each index in its OWN market from the home cash close of T−2 to the home cash close of T+3, McConnell & Xu's [−1, +3] (their window "begins with the last trading day"). It supersedes the branch's 14:30-New-York entry (R1-B's text: the UK, EU, Japan and Australia legs skipped day −1) and PLAN_R1's literal "T−1 close" (all six skipped it). Chosen on the papers' text before any D3 P&L was seen. Trading it forward later needs a cash-close fill slot, index onboarding ([[GAP-P12D-27]]) and the venue's weekend-holding check.
- **Found 2026-10-03 (second session), measured on the candles:** the index CFDs trade through exchange holidays (US500 on 4 July, Memorial and Labor Days until 13:00 New York; JP225 on 2–3 January and through Golden Week; UK100 on bank holidays; EU50 on 1 May), so the branch's "a weekday with a traded cash-session hour" mis-dated about 1–2 month-turns a year per index. Windows now come from each exchange's own holiday rules, cross-checked against an outside calendar library.
- **BUILT 2026-10-03 (second session):** `exchange_days.py` (month-turn holiday rules for XNYS, XLON, XEUR, XTKS and XASX, refusing any day outside the vetted scope). It was verified against `exchange_calendars` 4.13.2 on every in-scope day of 2012-01..2024-10:
  - NYSE, LSE and ASX: 0 disagreements;
  - Tokyo: 1, the TSE outage of 2020-10-01, now a SPECIAL closure;
  - Xetra: 10 (Whit Monday 2015–2020, German Unity Day 2014 and 2016–2019, Reformation Day 2017). These are Xetra-only closures: the EU50 CFD traded full sessions, and Paris, Amsterdam, Madrid and Milan were open, so XEUR keeps Eurex's list.

  Also built: `event_sim.py` rewritten to the ruling (C8 `per_side_cost`, fixed units, skip reasons, the passive-long context), `evaluate_events.py` (register first, kill-only), the scope widened, and `scripts/r1_d3_kill_test.py`.
- **Dry check (P&L-blind, `--dry-check`, 2026-10-03):** 140 graded turns per index (2012-04 to 2023-11). The anchors sit on the hour ending at each cash close: US 15:00 New York, UK 15:00 London, EU 16:00 Berlin, Tokyo 14:00, Sydney 15:00. US half days anchor at 13:00, the CFD's close about 15 minutes after the early bell. Three partial-feed days anchor early (US500 2013-09-27 at 11:00; US500 and US30 2013-10-03 at 12:00), declared. Turns without an anchor are skipped with their reason: US30 before 2013-09-30 and AUS200 before 2013-01 (no data), plus feed holes of US500 5, UK100 4, JP225 13, AUS200 9 and EU50 1 ([[GAP-P12D-156]]).
- Proof: `test_each_exchange_closes_on_its_own_holidays`, `test_a_month_turn_is_t_minus_2_to_t_plus_3_on_the_exchanges_own_sessions`, `test_a_day_outside_the_vetted_scope_or_years_is_refused`, `test_each_event_runs_from_the_cash_close_of_t_minus_2_to_that_of_t_plus_3`, `test_a_legs_daily_pieces_sum_to_its_weight_times_its_net_and_costs_book_per_side`, `test_a_legs_weight_reads_no_price_after_its_entry`, `test_a_turn_without_data_vol_or_an_anchor_is_skipped_with_its_reason`, `test_a_planted_turn_of_month_effect_earns_net_of_cost_and_a_null_does_not`, `test_an_event_grade_writes_its_line_first_then_chains_its_outcome_and_vectors`, `test_a_grade_that_cannot_honestly_run_is_refused_before_any_line`
- **THE LOOK (2026-10-03, look `000005`, outcome `000006`, both anchored on GitHub `look-anchor`; `scripts/r1_d3_kill_test.py` at `5fe7a0f`; register copied to `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/validation/2026-10-03c/`):** 780 events, 2012-04 to 2023-12. 60 turns were skipped: 29 for no data or vol warm-up, 31 for feed holes. Net annual Sharpe **−0.002**, HAC t **−0.01**: NOT killed (the line is −0.5). A survival credits nothing: the look is kill-only.
  - **Context, P&L-honest:** the passive long on the same legs earned 0.44, so the turn-of-month window earned LESS per day than the average day (R1-B's own G2 test would have killed it). At 2× cost it earns −0.08. That is consistent with the post-2008 decay the literature reports.
  - **Per index (post-hoc, never a selection):** US500 +0.17% per event (t 1.05), US30 +0.12% (0.72), UK100 +0.08% (0.48), EU50 −0.07% (−0.33), JP225 −0.06% (−0.26), AUS200 −0.09% (−0.61). The US legs together give t 1.26, the rest −0.35; 2012–17 was negative and 2018–23 positive. Choosing the US legs now would be a new family-version refined after this look: kill-only again, and a forking path.
- Residual: GAP-P12D-156 — the candle store's zero-volume quotes, found by this harness's dry check. Otherwise none: D3 is dropped (no forward test, no US-only version), the index onboarding stays shelved ([[GAP-P12D-27]]), and the event harness stays as the grader for the next scheduled-window rule.
- Justification: closed by the owner's ruling on the look's result: a mechanism that earns nothing on history does not earn its builds (onboarding, a cash-close slot, the venue's weekend check).
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-151]], [[GAP-P12D-27]], [[GAP-P12D-127]], [[GAP-P12D-156]]
