## GAP-P12D-148: the 2026-10-02 research pass (five agents) — why the engine loses, what winners do, what prop traders make: findings and triage
- Severity: high · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (2026-10-02: five agents on the owner's go — "i need agents launched to do thorough research and deduce what might be causing our engine to be losing thus far … you can launch up to 5 agents each with different privileges"; triaged by the lead; owner decisions and verification remainders below)
- Area: reports `archive/RESEARCH_FORENSICS_2026-10-02.md` (repo + server, no web), `archive/RESEARCH_PRACTITIONERS_2026-10-02.md` (web), `archive/RESEARCH_PROP_RETURNS_2026-10-02.md` (web), `archive/RESEARCH_GATES_2026-10-02.md` (repo + server + web), `archive/RESEARCH_EXITS_2026-10-02.md` (web); their probes and runs: the session scratchpad `research/`, the server `/opt/acis/research-forensics/data/forensics/runs/`, `/opt/acis/gates_out/`
- Description (what survived the lead's checks; nothing below binds as an edge claim):
  1. **Why the legacy engine loses (FORENSICS F1–F8).** It loses on holding period and exits, not entry direction. Entries are right 52–60% of the time, but the 1-minute ATR brackets hold 6–9 minutes and earn 0.01–0.11 bps of drift against ~0.8–1.0 bps of cost. NVMA rations trades (vetoes 39–46% of the time) without selecting better ones (direction 51%); QIEA trades 40–67% less at the same per-trade result; the factor sizing can move size away from the module with the edge; TSMOM's −$57 is mostly a bad path (its rule alone: −$2 ± $13 over 400 seeds) but it locks instruments and floods orders. Wider exits (no take-profit, a 5× stop) turn BREAKOUT net-positive on a market whose causal ceiling is ~4.5. R1.6 already drops all of these except one gate ([[GAP-P12D-147]]).
  2. **The positive control was unwinnable (FORENSICS F1, verified).** On the C-BT-12 planted market (oracle Sharpe 10, 1–4 h episodes) the best causal rule nets a Sharpe of 0.15 / 0.34 against −0.08..0.09 on the null; §14 asks for 1.5. GAP-P12D-49/31's "the engine fails its own positive control" therefore measures friction and variance, not trend capture. Harness changes owed: grade engines against markets whose causal ceiling clears the bar (`ceiling.py`), and fix the synthetic tape's proportional spread (it fires NVMA's ILLIQUID rule on price rises only, ~27.5 points of the veto rate).
  3. **The gates starve more than they protect (GATES).** On the lab with a planted trend, a $10k live account with every gate as built keeps 12–15% of the gate-free return; the notional cluster cap, the one-lot cap/refused adds, the currency cap and the undiversified VaR cap cost the most, and each lowers return per unit of drawdown. The VaR and cluster checks can raise human-only global halts (16–40% of 10-year lab runs). GATES's design (R1–R10: a 1%-of-capital daily risk budget, a cushion throttle min(budget, room/4), a whole-book forecast-risk ceiling instead of notional/VaR caps, nearest-lot rounding, self-clearing instrument halts, a flatten at 60% of the firm's daily room, a relative equity floor, a shadow-book edge monitor, a slot-window retry, gated adds) keeps ~95% of the gate-free return and cuts the 10-year chance of touching a 10% max loss from 72% to 4% on the no-edge lab. Use the budget to scale DOWN only (scaling up raised null failures to 18%). R1/R2 are G-1/G-3 of the ratified post-edge plan; R3–R9 need the owner, after rung 1 ([[GAP-P12D-61]]).
  4. **Exits (EXITS).** Keep R1.6's shape: tight stops turn edge into spread (Kaminski & Lo 2014; Clare et al. 2013, "a change of trend is the best stop loss"); "tight on losing" means sized loss, not a tight stop. Before a funded account: cap each position's loss at its stop (~1–1.5% of capital; at live sizing a 10 × ATR stop is ~4.3% on FX, 7.8–15.6% on metals); move the US-index slot off 10:00 ET releases (E8's news ban); model the disaster stop and no-re-entry in RSIM ([[GAP-P12D-141]] (11)); settle same-side adds.
  5. **What prop traders make (PROPRETURNS).** Paid funded traders take ~2–6% of the account per payout over 3–6 weeks; the durable top tier makes ~0.35–0.7% a week; only ~1–7% of buyers are ever paid (E8's own pass rate 17.7%). A realistic target for ACIS is **~0.2% of the account a week (0.1–0.3%)**, needing a real Sharpe of ~0.65–0.85, judged over 6–12 months (44–47% of weeks lose even with an edge). On E8 One the chosen drawdown option is the biggest lever (8% over the 4% preset); E8 One bans opening, closing and stop/target fills within ±5 minutes of high-impact news on funded accounts (profits there are removed), so the event calendar ([[GAP-P12D-03]]) precedes the funded account.
  6. **What winners do (PRACTITIONERS).** Slow, few-parameter rules on daily bars with vol targeting, many independent bets, passive-first execution, and no equity-curve filters; live records sit at a Sharpe of ~0.3–0.8 (bank-built strategies: 1.20 backtest → 0.31 live). 13 FX pairs are only ~2.25 independent bets (all 21 instruments ≈ 4.8): breadth must come from metals and indices ([[GAP-P12D-27]]) and from different mechanisms, each on its own forward clock. Integer-aware book selection (Carver's greedy minimum-tracking-error method) is the known answer to the small-account lot problem.
- **Verified by the lead (2026-10-02):** FORENSICS's baseline replication (−70.84 net, −55.19 direction, −15.65 friction = GAP-P12D-49) and its F1 ceiling, re-run on the server (planted 0.15 / 0.34, null −0.08 / 0.09); GATES's alpha-decay lockout and guard-attribution claims and PRACTITIONERS'/FORENSICS's NVMA-on-target-entries claim (code read); E8 One's ±5-minute news ban (E8's help page, via search); Harvey et al. (JPM 2018: vol targeting has a negligible Sharpe effect on currencies).
- **PROPRETURNS's Monte Carlo VERIFIED 2026-10-03 by the lead** (Appendix A's code unchanged, 20,000 paths a cell on the server, 36 s; `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/verify/propreturns_grid.json`): every grid cell is within Monte Carlo error of the report (8%/0.75: P(pass) 0.265/0.43/0.62 and EV per $10k −57/+33/+224 at S = 0/0.5/1.0, against 0.28/0.46/0.64 and −55/+34/+239). One correction: in the slow validation cell (S 0.5, k 0.25%) the simulation sits BELOW the closed form (0.432 vs 0.456), not above as the report says; the 2-year horizon truncates late passes. Added at D1's measured history Sharpe S = 0.16 ([[GAP-P12D-151]]): EV is negative per $10k in every cell (best −$29 at 8%/1.0) and positive per $100k on the 8% option (+$173 at 0.75, +$243 at 1.0).
- **Not yet verified (owed before any of it binds):** FORENSICS's ablation tables (p10/m30/l10) and a second TSMOM seed (~80 min); GATES's lab percentages and its design's 95%; the remaining literature in PRACTITIONERS and EXITS; E8 One's drawdown-option prices and whether it allows weekend holding.
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-02 (the weekly target):** "0.2%/week over 6–12 months (Recommended)". ACIS aims at ~0.2% of the account a week (range 0.1–0.3%) on a funded account, judged over 6–12 months against a zero-edge baseline, never week by week; money grows with account size and count, not risk. It sizes the post-edge risk budget ([[GAP-P12D-61]]).
- Justification: open. The survivors redirect work already ruled (R-1 retires QIEA/NVMA/AGON from money paths; R6-3 keeps them off the demo). The owner decisions they raise: [[GAP-P12D-145]], [[GAP-P12D-146]], [[GAP-P12D-147]] (all three BUILT, master v2.29/v2.30, 2026-10-02/03), the weekly target (ruled), and the post-edge risk design ([[GAP-P12D-61]]). The owed harness changes and the four no-ratification builds (a realised-daily-vol report, the demo's gate-zero and slot-veto counts, a shadow-book P&L stream, adds as gated entries) are in TODO. **2026-10-03: three of the four BUILT** — every `TARGET_DECIDED` now logs the uncapped continuous position (`shadow`, `core/governor/target.optimum_units`), the decision close and the quote's USD rate, and `scripts/demo_report.py` turns the logs into the refusal counts, the shadow book's daily USD P&L (swap-free, cost-free) and its realised vol against the target (`test_refusals_and_the_shadow_books_daily_pnl`, `test_the_shadow_position_is_the_banded_sizers_optimum`); adds as gated entries stay moot under the one-lot cap. FORENSICS's legacy ablation and TSMOM seed re-runs are DEPRIORITISED (the legacy modules are off the money path, GAP-P12D-153), not cancelled.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-49]], [[GAP-P12D-31]], [[GAP-P12D-61]], [[GAP-P12D-142]], [[GAP-P12D-94]], [[GAP-P12D-03]]

## GAP-P12D-147: an NVMA veto read from 1-minute bars can block a daily target entry at its slot, and the slot is not retried
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed (2026-10-03, owner ruling "Exempt confidence vetoes (Recommended)"; master v2.30, ACIS-05 v1.9 §6.4)
- Area: core/governor/guards.py (`not regime.tradeable` → `veto`), core/regime/nvma.py:347 (confidence < 0.55 → `LOW_CONFIDENCE` veto), core/runtime/target_loop.py `execute` (one attempt per slot), ACIS-05 §6.4 ("an increase … meets every entry gate"), master §5.5 ("regimes no longer gate families")
- Description: the target path's daily decision is executed at the symbol's slot only if NVMA's classification of the last few 1-minute bars is tradeable at that instant. A low-confidence or ILLIQUID reading zeroes the entry (`TARGET_ENTRY_ZERO` `veto`); the next attempt is the next day's decision. GATES measured 42–44% of 08:00 New York slots blocked on synthetic ticks (probably inflated by the generator's constant-fraction spread) but only −0.03 to −0.18 pp/yr of lab return, because the entry recurs the next day. The real-tick rate is unmeasured. RSIM models no veto (GAP-P12D-141 (10)). Verified by the lead in code (2026-10-02).
- Options: (a) measure first: count the demo's `TARGET_ENTRY_ZERO` reasons at the slot for two weeks (P&L-blind; no ruling needed); (b) retry inside the slot's grace window (`slot_grace_s`, 5 min) while the veto lasts; (c) exempt target-path entries from the confidence-based vetoes, keeping the gate states (stale, spread, dead market, macro event); (d) model the gate in RSIM. Lead's recommendation: (a) now, then rule (b) or (c) with the numbers.
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-02:** "Exempt confidence vetoes (Recommended)". Target-path ENTRIES ignore NVMA's confidence and label vetoes (`LOW_CONFIDENCE`, `NO_PARAMS_FOR_REGIME`, and the regime label itself); NVMA's data-integrity gates still apply (stale state, the spread veto, a dead market, a macro event, warm-up, NaN indicators). To build: an ACIS-05 §6.4 / ACIS-04 §4b amendment (master v2.29) saying which vetoes gate a target entry; the gate reads the veto reasons, not `tradeable`; a test that a low-confidence context admits a target entry and a stale or spread-vetoed one does not. The signal path is unchanged.
- **Fix 2026-10-03:** `RegimeContext.integrity_tradeable()` (core/regime/regime_types.py, `CONFIDENCE_VETOES`) and `EntryGuards.check(…, target=True)`, which the target gate alone passes (core/governor/target_gate.py): the `veto` reads the recorded reasons and the `low_confidence` guard is skipped (it reads the same confidence, so exempting only NVMA's veto would have changed nothing). Kept as integrity gates: the three gate states (UNCLASSIFIED covers warm-up and NaN indicators; MACRO_EVENT; ILLIQUID, which the code already treats as a gate state, GAP-0031, and which is spread- or closed-session-based) and STALE_STATE, SPREAD_VETO, DEAD_MARKET; a veto with no recorded reasons stays a veto. `NO_PARAMS_FOR_REGIME` is safe to skip: the target loop refuses an unregistered family on its own (`core/runtime/target_loop.py`, §2's rule-set row). The signal path passes no flag and keeps every veto.
- **Correction 2026-10-03 ([[GAP-P12D-150]]):** a target entry also needs a regime context at most two NVMA cycles old (`stale_context`, PR16I-06), and ILLIQUID's place in the keep-list is the lead's reading, an owner decision (PR16I-03).
- Residual: GAP-P12D-141 — (10): the entry guards RSIM does not model; the demo's `TARGET_ENTRY_ZERO` reasons measure what still blocks target entries after this fix (TODO START HERE item 3).
- Justification: fixed. Before the ruling the tradeable veto was HALT/veto semantics (CLAUDE.md), so nothing changed until the owner ruled.
- Linked: former `test_a_target_entry_ignores_the_confidence_vetoes_but_not_the_integrity_gates` (superseded 2026-10-03 by the feed watch: `test_a_target_entry_reads_the_feed_check_and_no_regime`, GAP-P12D-152), [[GAP-P12D-141]], [[GAP-P12D-148]]

## GAP-P12D-146: AUD's overnight rate comes from BIS's weekly release, which lags more than R6-1's 10-day freshness rule allows most weekdays
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain (on BIS's published cadence)
- Status: fixed (2026-10-02, owner ruling "21 days for weekly sources (Recommended)"; master v2.29, ACIS-04 v1.7 §4b R6-1)
- Area: core/data/rates.py `CCY_SERIES` (AUD = BIS WS_CBPOL area AU), config `targets.rate_max_stale_days: 10`, scripts/download_macro.py `--live`
- Description: BIS releases its policy-rate data weekly, around mid-week; its 2026-10-01 release carried AU through 2026-09-24. Under the 10-day rule AUD_USD, AUD_JPY and EUR_AUD get `no_target:stale_rates` on about 3 of 5 decision days (reproduced from 2026-10-05 with the real file). RSIM, with complete rates, decides all 5: an undeclared B0(d) divergence in 3 of 13 instruments. CHF (SNB, weekly on Mondays) keeps one day of margin. The live download now validates only the BIS area the engine reads, so a lagging foreign area can no longer block AU (fixed 2026-10-02, `test_live_mode_validates_only_the_bis_areas_the_engine_reads`).
- Options: (a) a policy-target rule: a central bank's policy rate is exact between its scheduled meetings, so it stays fresh until the next scheduled RBA decision (the 10 days stay for traded overnight rates); (b) a per-source limit sized to its cadence (21 days for weekly sources); (c) a daily AUD source (the RBA's F1.1 CSV refused scripted downloads from the laptop and the server on 2026-10-02). Lead's recommendation: (b), the simplest honest rule.
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-02:** "21 days for weekly sources (Recommended)". Each rate source's stale limit matches its publication cadence: 21 days for the weekly ones (BIS for AUD, SNB's cube for CHF), 10 for the daily overnight rates. To build: a per-currency limit in `core/data/rates.py` (`RateTable(max_stale_days=…)` per currency), config `targets.rate_max_stale_days` per source, the R6-1 amendment in ACIS-04 §4b (master v2.29); a test that AUD 18 days old is fresh and USD 11 days old is stale.
- **Fix 2026-10-02:** `core/data/rates.py` `WEEKLY_SOURCES` (bis_policy, chf_saron) and `RateTable(per_ccy=…)`; `load_rate_table(…, weekly_stale_days)`; config `targets.rate_max_stale_days_weekly: 21` (schema le 21), wired in `core/runtime/target_wiring.py`. RSIM's and the harness's callers keep the single 10-day limit (complete historical rates).
- **Correction 2026-10-03 (PR16I-05 / PR16E-03, [[GAP-P12D-150]]):** the `Residual: none` above missed `scripts/download_macro.py --live`, which still held the weekly sources to 10 days; FIXED the same day (`test_a_weekly_source_is_held_to_its_own_freshness_limit_in_live_mode`). BIS's year-end gap (PR16E-02) is an owner decision in [[GAP-P12D-150]].
- Residual: none — the engine's and the downloader's limits now read one config; the year-end gap is GAP-P12D-150's.
- Justification: fixed. Before the ruling, the AUD pairs decide on 1–2 days a week; nothing unsafe follows (no target keeps the position on its stop).
- Linked: test_a_weekly_source_stays_fresh_for_21_days_and_a_daily_one_for_10, [[GAP-P12D-141]], [[GAP-P12D-144]]

## GAP-P12D-145: the alpha-decay throttle can lock a one-lot book out of the market permanently, and a human resume does not clear it
- Severity: high · Likelihood: medium (it needs the detector armed: enough closed trades with decayed performance)
- Status: fixed (2026-10-03, owner rulings "Human resume clears it (Recommended)" 2026-10-02 and "Fresh evidence only (Recommended)" 2026-10-03; master v2.30, ACIS-07 v1.4 §7)
- Area: core/governor/target_gate.py `_entry_size` (× 0.25, then the one lot rule toward zero), core/risk/monitor.py:178-188 (the flag clears only when the detector reads recovery from NEW closed trades), :304-313 (`on_human_resume` re-arms the halt latches and clears halts, not alpha decay), config/guardrail_limits.yaml `alpha_decay_sharpe_mult`
- Description: once armed, every target entry under the paper/practice one-lot cap is 0.25 lot, which rounds to zero (`alpha_decay`). With no new entries, the book only closes, so the trades that could clear the throttle stop coming. GATES found the throttle still armed at the end of 10-year lab runs in 38–64% of runs in the demo configuration. The legacy path behaves differently: there the fixed one lot overwrites the throttled size, so the same Risk signal is ignored on one path and absolute on the other.
- Options: (a) a human resume also clears the alpha-decay flag (logged; Risk re-arms it if decay persists); (b) under a one-lot cap the throttle halves the cap's frequency rather than zeroing (e.g. entries alternate), keeping a trickle of trades; (c) replace alpha decay with GATES's R8, an edge monitor on a shadow book that keeps trading at minimum size. Lead's recommendation: (a) now, (c) with the post-edge guardrail plan.
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-02:** "Human resume clears it (Recommended)". The one human resume (`CapitalGovernor.resume` → `RiskMonitor.on_human_resume`) also clears the alpha-decay throttle, logged (`ALPHA_DECAY_CLEARED_BY_HUMAN`); Risk re-arms it at its next evaluation if decay persists. GATES's shadow-book edge monitor replaces it later, with the post-edge plan. To build: an ACIS-07 amendment (master v2.29); a test that a resume clears an armed throttle and a persisting decay re-arms it. A relaxation only a human can make, so the guard law holds.
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-03 (asked while building):** the 2026-10-02 text "Risk re-arms it at its next evaluation if decay persists", read literally, re-arms it within a second on the same trades (entries are zeroed while armed, so no new trades arrive), and the resume would do nothing in the lock-out case. Owner: "Fresh evidence only (Recommended)": after a human clear the detector reads only post-resume trades as recent, against the baseline that armed it, and re-arms once `alpha_decay_min_trades` (30) of them show the decay.
- **Fix 2026-10-03:** `AlphaDecayDetector.clear_by_human` + the fresh-evidence window in `evaluate` (core/risk/alpha_decay.py); the throttle's edge to the Governor moved out of monitor.py into `core/risk/alpha_throttle.py` (set, recover, restore, and `clear_by_human`, logging `ALPHA_DECAY_CLEARED_BY_HUMAN` and sending the explicit `apply_risk_state(alpha_decay=False)`), called from `RiskMonitor.on_human_resume` after the token check; monitor.py 343 → 332 lines. ~~A restart needs nothing persisted~~ (WRONG, PR16I-01: the arming baseline was lost with the trade history; corrected 2026-10-03, [[GAP-P12D-150]]: the frozen arming baseline is persisted and judges the post-resume trades until they form their own baseline).
- Residual: GAP-P12D-61 — the post-edge guardrail plan, whose shadow-book edge monitor (GATES R8) replaces this throttle.
- Justification: fixed. A relaxation only the one human resume can make, so the guard law holds; before the ruling it could not bite until the detector armed.
- Linked: test_a_human_resume_clears_the_throttle_and_only_post_resume_decay_re_arms_it, test_a_human_resume_leaves_an_unarmed_throttle_alone, [[GAP-P12D-61]], [[GAP-P12D-148]], [[GAP-P12D-139]]

## GAP-P12D-144: the R1.6 dual audit (2026-10-02) — 19 findings (13 internal, 6 external), triaged by the lead
- Severity: high · Likelihood: certain
- Status: mitigated (2026-10-02: every finding verified by the lead against the code; 14 fixed with a regression test, 1 mitigated, 2 declared, 2 owner decisions; the residual is owned below)
- Area: R1.6 at `de96a75`; reports `archive/AUDIT_R1_6_INTERNAL_2026-10-02.md`, `archive/AUDIT_R1_6_EXTERNAL_2026-10-02.md`; brief `archive/AUDIT_BRIEF_R1_6_2026-10-02.md` (owner's go: "Launch both now (Recommended)")
- Description and dispositions:
  - **R16I-01 = R16E-01 (high): the target path would stop ~101 days after its first seed.** The live rates were a rolling 1,200-day window and the cache is never trimmed. FIXED: `--live` fetches from 2011-01-01 and validates each series' start (`test_the_live_window_starts_at_the_research_start_so_it_covers_any_cache`).
  - **R16I-05 (high): a slot's entries were judged against one pre-slot portfolio,** so at $10k NAV all 13 opens would cross four clusters and raise a human-only HALT. FIXED: opens in flight count in every exposure (`Executor.inflight`, `build_portfolio(inflight=…)`; `test_opens_in_flight_count_in_every_exposure_so_one_slot_cannot_cross_a_cap`). It also closes the same hole on the legacy path.
  - **R16I-02 (medium): ACIS-13 A8 never ran on the target universe.** FIXED: A8 runs on every live tick, a decision waits (and retries) until some clock is proven, and an entry needs its own instrument's (`test_the_decision_waits_for_a_proven_clock_and_an_entry_needs_its_own`).
  - **R16I-03 (medium): a restart after a disaster stop-out re-entered before the next decision.** FIXED: the day's held position is persisted (`HistoryStore.meta`), and a changed book gives `no_target:position_changed_since_decision` (`test_a_restart_after_a_stop_out_does_not_re_enter_before_the_next_decision`).
  - **R16I-04 (low): a failed fetch still decided on yesterday's close.** FIXED: `no_target:fetch_failed` (`test_a_failed_fetch_gives_no_target_even_with_a_populated_cache`).
  - **R16I-06 (medium): the 17:00 decision blocks the event loop** (3.4 s at first boot, 11.1 s at 10 years on 16 vCPU). MITIGATED: it yields between instruments, so the loop is held for at most one instrument's share. Residual below.
  - **R16I-07, R16I-09 (A16): two load-bearing claims had no test.** FIXED: `test_the_quote_rate_converts_each_quote_to_usd_through_its_usd_leg_and_sizes_with_it` (q pinned by hand on a JPY quote) and `test_the_legacy_max_hold_never_closes_a_target_universe_position` (with its control). The GAP-P12D-118 proof pointer is repointed.
  - **R16I-08 (low): one missing rate file stopped all 13 pairs.** FIXED: a missing file stales only its currency (`test_a_missing_rate_file_stales_only_its_currency`).
  - **R16I-10 (latent): the one-lot cap was not re-applied at the slot.** FIXED (`test_the_mode_cap_is_reapplied_at_the_slot`).
  - **R16I-11 (low): undeclared RSIM departures** (RSIM trades on carried closes; it models no entry guard or slot skip). DECLARED as GAP-P12D-141 (9) and (10).
  - **R16I-12 = R16E-04 (low): `trend_d1_v1`'s record omitted the disaster stop, the warm-up mask, the bid basis and the practice cap.** FIXED before any registration (no durable store holds it: the demo never ran R1.6 code, and real looks are closed): `core/signals/families.py` `_SHARED`.
  - **R16I-13 (low): the narrowed fixture hid the §7.5 gate test's failure** (the mock face mapped 2 of 13), and a config error was a traceback. FIXED: the mock face maps the shipped universe and the gate test reads `shipped_config` (`test_symbol_map_covers_enabled_universe`); `__main__` refuses a config error with exit 2 (`ENGINE_CONFIG_REFUSED`).
  - **R16E-02 (medium): AUD's BIS source is weekly.** OWNER DECISION: [[GAP-P12D-146]]. The live download now validates only AU.
  - **R16E-03 (medium): the H1 walk used 365-day windows, beyond Spotware's once-documented 35 weeks, and trusted `hasMore`.** FIXED: 240-day windows; coverage judged from the bars; an empty window ends the walk; a history with a > 5-day gap gives `no_target:history_hole`, logged critical (`test_a_silently_truncated_response_is_walked_past_and_an_empty_window_ends_the_walk`, `test_the_default_window_is_inside_spotwares_documented_h1_range`, `test_a_history_with_a_hole_gives_no_target`).
  - **R16E-05 (low): the carry calendar's simplifications were undeclared.** DECLARED in `core/data/rates.py` and the TREND records.
  - **R16E-06 (low): GAP-P12D-141 (3) understated the short-history normaliser's range.** RESTATED there (0.87–1.11).
- Residual (owned here until each lands):
  - R16I-06: measure the decision's blocking time on the AWS t3.small at the cutover. The durable fix is an incremental per-instrument daily series (append one day per decision instead of rebuilding from H1), which needs a B0(d) re-proof.
  - ACIS-13 v1.4 §6.5's "a one-time ~4 requests per instrument" becomes ~6 with 240-day windows (an editorial correction for the owner's next spec pass).
  - The pre-cutover probe adds a 3-year H1 fetch on the bound account: it answers the venue's range behaviour and whether `registrationTimestamp` limits trendbar history for a NEW $100k account (R16E "could not verify").
- Justification: every finding was re-derived by the lead from the code paths the auditors cited, not taken on their word. The two owner decisions do not block the cutover; R16I-05's fix removes the "$100k account is a hard precondition" concern, though the owner's $100k ruling stands.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-141]], [[GAP-P12D-142]], [[GAP-P12D-146]], [[GAP-P12D-118]]

## GAP-P12D-143: the target path's daily history does not fit the venue request budget at the committed cadences (owner decision)
- Severity: high · Likelihood: certain
- Status: fixed (2026-10-02: the owner chose "Reconcile every 30 min (Recommended)"; applied as ACIS-13 v1.4 and config `reconcile_interval_s: 1800`, master v2.28)
- Area: specs/ACIS-13-VENUE_ADAPTERS.md §6.5 (A9's projection), ACIS-04 v1.6 §4b (venue-seeded history), config/system_config.yaml `venue:`
- Description: ACIS-04 v1.6 seeds and appends each traded instrument's daily history from the venue's H1 trendbars, a metered query: about 2 requests a day per instrument (the 17:00 append and a restart's gap fill). The first boot's 3-year seed is a one-time ~4 per instrument, refused inside the reserve, so it can delay a forecast but never an exit. The committed cadences already project exactly the 900 requests of room (8 × 11 + 576 polls + 96 reconciles + 140 orders), so 13 instruments (+26) or 21 (+42) cannot fit and A9 refuses the attach (`ATTACH_BUDGET_INFEASIBLE`). The projection now counts them (`ctrader_attach.feasible(targeted=…)`, `HISTORY_PER_INSTRUMENT = 2`); ACIS-13 §6.5's formula text does not carry the term yet.
- Options: (a) reconcile every 1,800 s, not 900 (−48 a day: 852 + 42 = 894 ≤ 900 for all 21); (b) poll equity every 360 s, not 300 (−96); (c) `max_orders_per_day` 35 → 25 (−40). Lead's recommendation: (a). A daily-decision book changes positions only at its slots, so the missing-stop scan moves from every 15 to every 30 minutes; the equity breaker's cadence is untouched.
- Residual: none — the budget fits with the history counted, and the first boot's deeper seed is a metered query refused inside the reserve (it can delay a forecast, never an exit); proof `test_a9_budgets_two_history_requests_per_targeted_instrument`, `test_the_order_allowance_counts_four_requests_a_round_trip`.
- Justification: fixed. The projection counts the history (`HISTORY_PER_INSTRUMENT = 2`) and the shipped cadences fit all 21 instruments: 8 × 11 + 576 + 48 + 140 + 42 = 894 ≤ 900. The equity poll is unchanged; the missing-stop scan runs every 30 minutes.
- Linked: test_a9_budgets_two_history_requests_per_targeted_instrument, [[GAP-P12D-57]], [[GAP-P12D-141]]

## GAP-P12D-142: on the $10k demo the hard caps admit about one one-lot FX position per cluster, so the realised book is a truncated, order-dependent subset of the graded one
- Severity: high · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (2026-10-02: RULED, "$100k demo, $10k sizing (Recommended)" and the demo vol target "20% (Recommended)", applied as ACIS-05 v1.8 and the `pepperstone_demo` rulebook, master v2.28; OWNER STEP DONE 2026-10-02: the existing demo topped up to $100,000; remaining: the R1.6 engine's first attach at the cutover must show sizing on capital_ref $10k, not on the $100k NAV)
- Area: core/governor/target_gate.py (§7.4 caps with G-5's headroom), core/runtime/target_loop.py (slot order), config/instruments.yaml `clusters`, tests/backtest/rsim.py (models no caps)
- Description: at $10,000 NAV, G-5's headroom admits 90% × 20% = $1,800 per cluster and 90% × 30% = $2,700 per currency. A one-lot (1,000-unit) FX position is $650–$1,330 of notional, so each cluster (usd_long, usd_short, european, yen_carry …) holds about one position and each currency about two in one direction. RSIM's practice book models no caps: B0(d) parity holds decision by decision, but the realised demo book drops whichever entries arrive after a cluster fills. All FX slots are 08:00 New York and run in instrument (alphabetical) order, so AUD_JPY, AUD_USD and CHF_JPY win the cap systematically. The forward record would measure a cap-truncated, alphabet-biased book.
- Options: (a) reset the Pepperstone demo to $100,000 with capital_ref kept at $10k (R6-5 unchanged; the caps then admit ~10 positions per cluster; the $9,000 equity floor becomes inert, on a demo only); (b) keep $10k and take the forward record from RSIM's practice book on forward venue data, the caps then shaping only the plumbing test; (c) order a slot's entries by |forecast| instead of by name (a new, unratified rule); (d) per-account guardrail profiles (the post-edge plan, not before rung 1).
- **Owner ruling 2026-10-02:** the demo account holds $100,000 while sizing keeps capital_ref $10k, now at a 20% vol target (J1: at 10% the minimum lot keeps the book flat ~78% of days). Each position is still at most one minimum lot. The owner opens, or resets, a Pepperstone cTrader demo at $100,000. A new account needs its id committed in `config/system_config.yaml` `venue.ctid_trader_account_id` and `config/watchdog.yaml`, the Spotware grant extended to it, and a read-only probe before the cutover (TODO START HERE item 4). Until then the $10k account stays bound and its caps truncate the book as described.
- **Correction 2026-10-02 (R16I-05):** the description above was wrong about the code. The slot judged every entry against the same pre-slot portfolio, so at $10k all 13 opens would have gone out and crossed four clusters, raising a human-only HALT, not a truncated book. FIXED: opens in flight now count in every exposure ([[GAP-P12D-144]]), so the caps do truncate as described; the owner's $100k ruling makes them inert on the demo.
- **Owner's route 2026-10-02:** TOP UP the existing demo (login 5350811, ctid 48971796) to $100,000 inside cTrader (Pepperstone: virtual funds can be topped up any time in cTrader; fallback support@pepperstone.com), not a new account, so no config, grant or token changes. A deposit is safe for the running engine: its `DEPOSIT_WITHDRAW` execution event is ignored by the router (`core/execution/ctrader_events.py:120`) and only an equity FALL trips a guard; the old engine's logs show it as one +$90k day (cosmetic; that book retires at the cutover). The next session's read-only probe confirms the balance.
- **Owner step DONE 2026-10-02 (~20:10 UTC):** the owner topped up login 5350811 (owner-reported balance 99,996.42). Confirmed read-only from the running engine's own venue poll, without a second venue connection: `data/runtime/risk_practice.db` `risk_snapshot` equity 9,996.42 at 19:00 UTC and 99,996.42 at 20:21 UTC; no halt, 0 positions, reconcile drift false. The probe was NOT run beside the live engine: it would share the engine's token store and venue DB. The H1-history facts ([[GAP-P12D-141]] (1), (2), (6)) are therefore read at the cutover's first seeding. Until the cutover, the OLD engine on acis-demo-1 (4e2d4b2) sizes from the venue NAV (`core/runtime/engine.py` `portfolio()`, no capital_ref), so its rare champion trades carry ~10x the notional at the same percentage risk: demo money, and that book retires at the cutover.
- Justification: open, owner-owned: the guardrail values are human-only and untouched; which book the forward record measures is a stage-D design call (with [[GAP-P12D-140]]).
- Linked: test_the_risk_edge_throttles_and_the_hard_caps_zero_a_one_lot_entry, [[GAP-P12D-61]], [[GAP-P12D-56]], [[GAP-P12D-140]]

## GAP-P12D-141: the target path's declared departures from RSIM, and the venue facts it assumes until the first seeding and attach
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: high
- Status: open (2026-10-02, R1.6 steps 3–4; (1), (2) and (6) are answered at the cutover's first attach and seeding and recorded in OPERATIONS_LEDGER.md)
- Area: core/data/ctrader/history.py, core/signals/daily.py, core/runtime/target_decision.py, config/instruments.yaml (the 11 newly enabled FX)
- Description: (1) cTrader trendbars are assumed to be BID bars (the chart convention), where RSIM grades Dukascopy MID closes: a half-spread offset, and rollover-time spread noise in the close (`OQ_TRENDBARS_BID`). (2) Spotware documented the H1 range limit at 35 weeks until proto release 88 (R16E-03): the fetch uses 240-day windows, judges coverage from the bars, and halves on a refusal (`TRENDBARS_WINDOW_HALVED`, logged). (3) The engine seeds ~3 years (`history_days: 1100`) where RSIM's history starts in 2012, so Carver's slow vol (a 2,560-day EWMA, 30% of the normaliser) averages fewer years: the normaliser measured 0.87–1.11 of the full-history one (median 0.98–1.01, R16E-06, P&L-blind), so forecasts and sizing vol differ by up to ~13% at the tails. (4) Sizing reads the quote's USD rate at the decision close; RSIM's `q` is the fill's. (5) A slot a closed market removes is skipped (ACIS-05 §6.4), where RSIM fills day t at the next traded slot, so on a holiday RSIM trades twice at one price and the engine once. (6) The 11 newly enabled FX pairs' contract values (5/3 digits, 100,000-unit lots, 0.01-lot step and minimum) are Pepperstone's standard, checked only at attach (`cross_check`, fatal on a stricter venue); their C8 costs are provisional. (7) `targets.idm: 1.5` is a declared prior (subsystem correlation 0.4 over 13 FX), not an estimate: the owner may re-rule it with R1-D14 (J1). (9) RSIM forecasts and trades on carried (stale) closes, where the engine makes no target (R6-1; R16I-11). (10) RSIM models none of the entry guards (NVMA veto, halts, the closure buffer, taper, alpha decay, `no_regime`, the A8 clock) nor the slot skips (busy, no mark, a changed position): the demo's `TARGET_ENTRY_ZERO` and `TARGET_SLOT_SKIPPED` counts measure the gap ([[GAP-P12D-147]]). (11) RSIM models neither the disaster stop nor the no-same-day-re-entry rule (EXITS R-1): a future stop change would go live ungraded. (8) A restart re-derives the same target from the same bars and rates; if the daily macro refresh adds the decision day's rate between the 17:02 decision and the restart, the accrual and the `inputs_hash` differ by that one day's carry (the rates are forward-filled until published).
- **2026-10-03 (GAP-P12D-159 PCE-05):** C8 declares a 0 FX commission, while Pepperstone's cTrader charges $6 per lot round trip on FX. Measure it with the spreads at the first attach; GOTOBI's draft G-6 and every forward cost check include it.
- Justification: open: each is declared in code and none moves a guardrail. (1), (2) and (6) are venue facts; (3)–(5) are recorded departures that RSIM can adopt or the engine can close later; (7) is sizing only, under the one-lot cap.
- Linked: test_decision_days_closes_and_staleness_equal_rsims_rules, test_b0d_the_engines_decisions_equal_rsims_practice_book_on_a_lab_tape, test_a_refused_range_halves_the_window_and_too_small_a_window_raises, [[GAP-P12D-101]], [[GAP-P12D-27]]

## GAP-P12D-140: the first cycle's testing design and B0(d)'s place: two owner rulings the first look needs
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: fixed (2026-10-03, owner rulings; 2026-10-01, from the PACE research pass, `archive/RESEARCH_PACE_AND_CREDITS_2026-10-01.md`)
- Area: ACIS-09 C-BT-11 v2 step 3 (serial gatekeeping, D-d) and step 0 (B0), TODO's R1 order
- Description: (1) cycle 1 spends α = 0.03 of the lifetime 0.05 on a fixed sequence led by D1, whose stage-A pass probability over 10.85 graded years is 0.20 / 0.48 / 0.76 at a swap-free net Sharpe of 0.32 / 0.555 / 0.79 (the lead re-derived it: Φ(SR·√Y − 1.881)) and about 0.05 at FTMO's swaps; PACE's simulation (unverified) says an online-fallback or weighted-Holm allocation keeps lifetime FWER <= 0.05 and gives 1.6–2.8× the expected true discoveries when the first family is uncertain. (2) B0 item (d), "RSIM ≡ the engine on lab tapes", needs R1.6, while TODO puts the first look right after R1.4: either (d) binds from stage B on (stage A may be graded before R1.6), or the first look waits for R1.6.
- **2026-10-02:** B0(d) is BUILT for D1 (`test_b0d_the_engines_decisions_equal_rsims_practice_book_on_a_lab_tape`: the engine's targets equal RSIM's practice book at every decision of a 620-day lab tape), so item (2)'s sequencing question is moot for the first look; (1) stands.
- **Owner rulings 2026-10-03:** (1) "D1 alone at 0.03 (Recommended)", then superseded the same day: D1 is LLM-selected under §5.6 ("LLM-selected, kill test (Recommended)"), so its 2012–2023 look is kill-only, names no cycle and spends no α; cycle 1's 0.03 stays free for the first family whose certifying boundary falls inside the history, under the fixed chain as built. (2) was moot already.
- Residual: none — no cycle row is due until a family with a historical certifying window is registered; the chain and the spending schedule are built (`tests/backtest/verdict.py`, `look_claims.cycle_line`).
- Justification: fixed by the owner's rulings; the allocation question returns only when a cycle holds several families.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-102]], [[GAP-P12D-111]], [[GAP-P12D-105]], [[GAP-P12D-94]]

## GAP-P12D-139: an instrument halt clears only by human resume in code, while ACIS-07 says it also clears when its condition clears
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: high
- Status: open (2026-10-01, from the SIZING research pass; HALT semantics: an owner ruling, human-only)
- Area: specs/ACIS-07-RISK_MONITOR.md §8 ("instrument halt clears when its condition clears *or* by human resume per policy"), the Governor's `_halts` and `PersistentHaltSink` (no clearing verb)
- Description: the code takes the stricter reading: a per-instrument 1% open-loss halt stays until `resume(human_token)`. On an unattended demo that is a starvation path (master §10's anti-paralysis rule), and with multi-week holds at >= 0.25% of NAV a day per instrument about a third of holds would touch it (reflection principle, SIZING §1.2).
- Justification: open: the stricter code is the safe side, so nothing is at risk today (the demo trades one minimum lot). The owner rules which reading is meant before a multi-week book trades; the spec and the code are then made to agree.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-61]], [[GAP-P12D-131]]

## GAP-P12D-138: the practice engine's logs and market-state store grow without bound, about 0.3–0.5 GB a day, and nothing rotates them
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (2026-10-01, measured on acis-demo-1 while sizing the AWS demo host)
- Area: logs/*.jsonl (the engine's structured logs), data/runtime/market_state.sqlite, the demo hosts' disks
- Description: in the 7 h since the practice start at 12:34 UTC the engine wrote 75 MB of logs (data_log.jsonl 53 MB, signal_log.jsonl 24 MB), about 0.26 GB a day, and `market_state.sqlite` stood at 58 MB (its growth rate is not yet measured separately). Nothing rotates, compresses or prunes either. The AWS demo host's 30 GB disk (the free-plan EBS allowance, with the OS and venv taking about 3 GB) therefore fills in roughly two months, and a full disk stops the JSONL writes the §12 plane and the operational ledger read.
- Justification: open, bounded: the demo trades a demo account and the watchdog flattens a silent engine, so a full disk ends in a flat, halted engine, not in a loss. Before about 2026-11-15: measure the store's growth on the AWS host over a day, then choose with the owner between rotation (logrotate with copytruncate, or the engine reopening its files), pruning the store, or a larger disk. The logs are the operational-referent audit's evidence, so rotated files are archived, never deleted.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-76]], [[GAP-P12D-12]]

## GAP-P12D-137: the energy CFDs (UKOIL, NATGAS, USOIL) carry unadjusted futures rolls, and their swap markup is unexplained
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (2026-10-01, found probing the R1.5 candles on the Google server)
- Area: tests/backtest/rsim_real.py (refuses energy), tests/backtest/swaps.py, tests/backtest/universe.py
- Description: Dukascopy's GASCMDUSD jumps 1.86 -> 2.55 inside 2020-09-22, a front-month roll it does not back-adjust; a trend rule reading it would trade the gap. Oil's largest daily moves (2020-03-09, 2020-04-21) are real crash days, but its roll handling is unverified too. Separately, FTMO's UKOIL swap reads as a 16%/yr markup under the symmetric reading (GAP-P12D-93), which the rolling contract's term structure, not a markup, probably explains.
- Justification: open, bounded: `rsim_real.build` REFUSES the energy class, so no grade can read an unadjusted roll; the first real-data look's explored universe is 21 instruments (23 less UKOIL and NATGAS) unless this closes first, an owner registration decision. To close: detect each roll (date and gap) from the candles or Dukascopy's published roll schedule, back-adjust the excess price additively as Carver does for futures, and decompose the energy swap into roll yield plus markup.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-93]], [[GAP-P12D-136]], [[GAP-P12D-94]]

## GAP-P12D-136: R1.3 is built for the lab; the real-data half of RSIM is still owed
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: open (2026-10-01; the owner chose "R1 build on the lab (Recommended)" to use the Google credits)
- Area: tests/backtest/rsim.py, rsim_lab.py, core/signals/rules.py, ewm.py, families.py, scripts/r1_lab_rsim.py
- Description: BUILT 2026-10-01 (`b154087` + the provenance commit): the TREND members as pure stdlib functions reproducing Carver's code (GAP-P12D-122 fixed) and MOP's rule (GAP-P12D-116 fixed); RSIM with Carver sizing, his buffer, the one lot rule, the single C8 cost model per fill, three financing worlds; the lab adapter; the provenance records; the multi-seed lab runner. OWED before any real-data look: (1) RSIM's REAL-data source: the excess-return price from spot plus the dated carry (RateTable), the fill price and spread at each symbol's execution clock from hourly bid/ask candles ([[GAP-P12D-95]]), `q` from cross rates ([[GAP-P12D-27]]); it needs R1.5's candles; (2) the venue swap table as markup data with its charge calendar ([[GAP-P12D-93]]); (3) the two cost filters ([[GAP-P12D-115]]); (4) the passive-benchmark arms registered beside each family (R1-D P6; `PASSIVE_LONG` exists as a member); (5) the registered amendment path for a provenance record already written (the TSMOM text, GAP-P12D-116's residual); (6) R1.2's residual `evaluate(plan, tier, span)`, the ONLY producer of a graded real-data number (writes the claim row first, stores the daily vector), which wraps RSIM; (7) an OWNER ruling on the C9 member set: master §5.6 says D1 COMBINES the three EWMAC speeds but that breakout 40/80/160 and MOP's rule are its MEMBERS, and C9 binds every member (E ≤ 0 refuses the family). The stage-A lab run takes the strict reading, C9 over all seven (`scripts/r1_lab_stage_a.py` `C9_MEMBERS`); the alternative is C9 over the three combined speeds, with breakout and MOP graded as separate family-versions. **OWNER RULED 2026-10-01: "All seven, strict (Recommended)", applied as master v2.26 §5 C9.**
- **Lab calibration 2026-10-01 (synthetic, machinery only; `scripts/r1_lab_rsim.py` at `b154087`, 1,000 seeds, 20 instruments, 13.3 graded years each; `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/runs/2026-10-01-r1-lab-rsim/`):** D1's net Sharpe minus the null's on the same seed is +0.32 / +1.19 / +0.65 / +2.25 (planted oracle 0.5 or 1.0 per instrument, half-life 40 or 160 days; se <= 0.01), and every TREND member and MOP earn on every planted kind. On the null the mean daily gross P&L is zero (t across seeds +0.21 D1, -0.46 EWMAC16/64, -0.53 BREAKOUT80); the mean of per-seed Sharpes reads -0.01 to -0.02 only because a seed's mean and its volatility correlate +0.65..+0.75 (an averaging artefact, checked). The one-sided t false-detect rate on the null is 3-5% at alpha 5%. Cost ~0.1%/yr at the lab's EUR/USD-class costs.
- **Stage A on the lab, 2026-10-01 (`scripts/r1_lab_stage_a.py` at `75d2878`, 500 seeds, alpha 0.03, strict C9 over all seven members; `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/runs/2026-10-01-r1-lab-stage-a/`):** pass rate on the null 2.6% (the HAC bound alone 2.6%: it holds its size); planted trend 1.0/h40 98.0%, 0.5/h160 64.6%, weak 0.5/h40 18.4%. The kill-only conditions cost 1-3 points of power (bound 99.0 / 67.4 / 20.0%), and the strict C9 set refuses mostly through MOP12 and EWMAC64/256 on the weak trend.
- **Progress 2026-10-01 (later, owner: "lets sort out the still owed part"):** (1) RSIM's REAL-data source BUILT for FX, metals and indices (`tests/backtest/rsim_real.py`, `candle_store.py`): the 17:00-New-York decision, each symbol's pre-registered execution slot (FX/metals 08:00 New York, US indices 10:00 New York, UK100 09:00 London, EU50/GER40 09:00 Frankfurt, JP225 10:00 Tokyo, AUS200 11:00 Sydney; GAP-P12D-95's clock), carry per rollover by the swap calendar (FX the dated differential; metals an assumed 0.25% lease; indices the funding rate only, dividends not on disk: DECLARED), q from cross rates, tier guards (held-out only in stage C, no post-2023 data outside the holdout look), energy refused ([[GAP-P12D-137]]); proven on a real 2012-2023 market (`test_the_real_candles_build_a_market_whose_fills_follow_their_decisions`, server only). (3) the cost filters BUILT ([[GAP-P12D-115]]). (6) `evaluate()` BUILT (`tests/backtest/evaluate.py`; `test_a_synthetic_grade_writes_its_line_first_then_its_outcome_and_vector`). R1.5's data: 4,979 instrument-months verified (0 problems after the envelopes were widened to real extremes), copies on the AWS data volume (`/data/acis/r15/data/dukascopy_candles`), `s3://acis-transfer-565257597310/candles/dukascopy_candles_2012-2025.tgz` (sha256 `84ccf938...351ca`), the Google server `/opt/acis/data/dukascopy_candles` and `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/data/`; the overnight rates at `/opt/acis/data/macro` and `gs://acis-compute-0929-data/data/macro_2012-2025.tgz`. STILL OWED: (2) the swap table's energy and CFD calendars ([[GAP-P12D-137]], GAP-P12D-93); (4) registering the passive-benchmark arms with the family (at the first look's registration); (5) the provenance amendment path; and the per-instrument admission run of the cost filters on the real candles (a P&L-blind computation: spreads and vol only).
- **Progress 2026-10-01 (evening):** (4) BUILT: the claim declares its passive benchmark (`PASSIVE_LONG`, the RSIM arm the lab runs) with the rest of E-R12-03's declarations, sticky per variant (`tests/backtest/look_declarations.py`; proof in [[GAP-P12D-127]]). (5) BUILT by ACIS-11 §6's own rule, no spec change: a record is written once and a correction is a NEW id citing `lineage:<parent>`; TSMOM's corrected declaration is `tsmom_slow_v2` (registered 2026-10-01), and a store holding `tsmom_slow_v1` keeps it exactly as written (`test_a_corrected_record_is_a_lineage_successor_and_the_parent_stays_as_written`, `test_a_fresh_store_registers_only_the_corrected_record`). (2) DECIDED AT ITS BINDING POINT: FTMO's triple-swap day for metals and cash CFDs is not stated by any primary source found (FTMO's blog states Wednesday-to-Thursday generally, with an FX example; third-party summaries disagree), so `swaps.py` keeps refusing those classes in the markup world. It binds only if R1-D9 picks FTMO's swaps at the first look; then the owner reads the MT5 symbol specification (an FTMO free trial) or rules a declared assumption. The cost filters do not need the day: a year's markup is the same whichever weekday carries the triple. STILL OWED: the admission run on the real candles, and the energy rolls ([[GAP-P12D-137]]).
- Justification: open, bounded: no real-data look is open (`REAL_LOOKS_OPEN` False until R1.4), and every lab number is synthetic, machinery-only (ACIS-09 C-BT-12).
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-93]], [[GAP-P12D-95]], [[GAP-P12D-137]], [[GAP-P12D-115]], [[GAP-P12D-27]], [[GAP-P12D-116]], [[GAP-P12D-122]]

## GAP-P12D-135: the engine has no stop path — Ctrl+C or SIGTERM ends it without closing its positions
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: high
- Status: fixed (2026-10-01; OWNER RULED the same day: a deliberate stop (SIGINT/SIGTERM) closes every position, halts, then exits; built first of the [[GAP-P12D-131]] batch; full suite 1214 passed, 6 skipped on the Google server; the flatten, the halt, the stop branch and the signal handler each reverted fail the tests)
- Area: core/runtime/__main__.py (no signal handling), deploy/systemd/acis-engine.service (systemd stops with SIGTERM)
- Description: found 2026-10-01 while walking the owner through the first practice start. A KeyboardInterrupt unwinds `_run` through its `finally` (session stop, lock release) with no flatten, and SIGTERM (systemd stop, VM shutdown) kills it outright. Open positions keep their venue stops and are adopted at the next start; with the watchdog running, a stop with no restart inside `watchdog_timeout_s` (30 s) is flattened by the watchdog instead, but a quick restart leaves them riding.
- Justification: fixed. `install_stop` (core/runtime/__main__.py) turns SIGINT/SIGTERM into `Runtime.request_stop`; `run()` then takes the stalled-loop path with reason `operator_stop`: freeze opens, close every held position, halt everything, drain the closes (30 s, inside systemd's 90 s stop timeout), exit 0. A position still held after the drain is logged `STOP_LEFT_POSITIONS` (critical) and the watchdog flattens it once the heartbeat goes stale. The halts persist, so the next start needs `--practice --resume` (deploy/README.md). A signal before the runtime is built keeps asyncio's default (nothing is held yet).
- Proof: `test_a_deliberate_stop_in_practice_closes_every_position_and_halts` (fake cTrader venue: an adopted position, flat AT THE VENUE after the stop), `test_a_signal_is_a_deliberate_stop` (a real SIGTERM and SIGINT to the process).
- Residual: GAP-P12D-76 — the running demo (acis-demo-1, started 12:34 UTC on the pre-fix code) gets the stop rule only at its next start on the new checkout, planned as the AWS move (TODO #4); until then a Ctrl+C there still leaves positions on their venue stops for the watchdog.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-131]], [[GAP-P12D-133]]

## GAP-P12D-134: the deploy units and runbook assume the system Python with the dependencies installed into it, which Debian 12 refuses
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: fixed (2026-10-01, found deploying the demo on Debian 12 / Python 3.11, fixed the same day in the reboot-proofing step the owner approved)
- Area: deploy/systemd/acis-engine.service, deploy/systemd/acis-watchdog.service (`ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3 ...`), deploy/README.md
- Description: both units run `/usr/bin/python3` with `PYTHONPATH=/opt/acis`, so the dependencies (pydantic, aiohttp, PyYAML, ...) must be importable by the system interpreter. Debian 12 marks it externally managed (PEP 668): `pip install` into it is refused, so on a clean host both units fail at import. The demo host runs from a venv (`/opt/acis/venv`) for this reason, outside the documented units.
- Justification: fixed. Both units run `/opt/acis/venv/bin/python`; the runbook creates the venv and installs pyproject's dependencies exactly as CI does; the watchdog env note names the three `ACIS_CTRADER_*` variables; `deploy/demo/` holds the demo host's drop-in (its checkout, user and env file) and the two owner helpers. The demo watchdog now runs as an ENABLED unit with that drop-in (survives a reboot).
- Proof: `test_each_unit_runs_the_venv_interpreter`, `test_the_demo_drop_in_resets_then_points_at_the_venv`, `test_each_demo_helper_parses`.
- Residual: none — the units, runbook and demo drop-in are tested; a real reboot of acis-demo-1 has not been exercised (it would end the practice run), so the first one should be watched.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-131]], [[GAP-P12D-76]]

## GAP-P12D-133: the watchdog's cTrader plugin reported a successful flatten as a failure — it checked for flat before the close had filled
- Severity: medium · Likelihood: certain
- Status: fixed (2026-10-01, found by ACIS-13 §14.2 case 8 on the Pepperstone demo, the run that built [[GAP-P12D-131]] (i))
- Area: scripts/watchdog_plugins/ctrader_flatten.py `_flatten`; master §8.6 layer 2; ACIS-13 §7.9
- Description: the plugin sent each close, then reconciled ONCE. cTrader answers a `ProtoOAClosePositionReq` with an execution event (accepted) before the fill, so the reconcile still showed the position and the plugin returned `ok=False` ("1 position(s) still open"), although the close went through (a second pass found the account flat). In service the watchdog would have logged `WATCHDOG_FLATTEN_FAILED` and confirmed only on the next poll (5 s later).
- Justification: fixed. `_settled` polls the reconcile every 0.25 s for up to `settle_s` (5 s) until flat; `ok` still requires an observed flat book. Re-run of case 8 on the demo: `flatten ok, positions_closed 1`, second pass flat (OPS-0001).
- Proof: `test_a_close_that_fills_after_its_acknowledgement_is_waited_for` (fake venue that acknowledges, then fills 0.3 s later); `test_a_flatten_that_leaves_a_position_open_is_never_reported_ok` still holds.
- Residual: none — a close that never fills still fails loudly after `settle_s`, and the watchdog retries on its next poll.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-131]], [[GAP-P12D-132]]

## GAP-P12D-132: the Track A dual audit (adapter, runtime, reconnect) — 33 findings (19 internal, 14 external); TRIAGED, and the four owner rulings (D1–D4) APPLIED
- Severity: high · Likelihood: certain
- Status: fixed 2026-09-30 (every finding fixed with a regression test, mitigated, or owned by GAP-P12D-131; D1–D4 owner-ruled and applied as ACIS-13 v1.2 with tests; full suite 1202 passed, 6 skipped on the Google server; 14 reverted-fix mutants, all killed) (2026-09-30: the constitution's two audits of Track A steps 3–5, launched on the owner's go ("Launch both now (Recommended)"), brief `AUDIT_BRIEF_TRACK_A_2026-09-30.md` on `86ea8d3`; reports `archive/AUDIT_TRACK_A_INTERNAL_2026-09-30.md` (TAI-01..19: 4 high, 10 medium, 5 low) and `archive/AUDIT_TRACK_A_EXTERNAL_2026-09-30.md` (TAE-01..14: 2 high, 8 medium, 4 low). Triaged the same day; full suite 1198 passed, 6 skipped on the Google server; each new runtime test was checked to FAIL with its fix reverted (10 mutants, all killed))
- Area: core/data/ctrader/, core/execution/ctrader_*.py, core/execution/orchestrator.py, core/runtime/, core/risk/monitor.py, scripts/acis_watchdog.py, config/system_config.yaml, deploy/
- Description: both lenses refute two of the lead's claims — every fill reaching Risk exactly once, and the request spend staying inside ACIS-13 §6.5 — and agree on one mechanism: at a restart, a reconnect or a budget edge the runtime mixed two sources of truth (or two accounts) in one Risk. Almost every failure failed toward a halt, which TAI-07 (no reachable human resume) made permanent; the exceptions were an unprotected position after a failed stop repair (TAI-04/TAE-10) and a blind equity breaker in the close reserve (TAI-01).
- Dispositions (tests in `tests/integration/test_track_a_adapter_fixes.py` and `tests/integration/test_track_a_runtime_fixes.py` unless named):
  - **TAI-01** fixed in part: the close path's reduce-only pre-check is metered `protect`, and the periodic reconcile skips the reserve, so a close reaches the venue in the reserve (`test_a_close_through_the_orchestrator_reaches_the_venue_in_the_close_reserve`). The blind equity breaker is **D2**; A9's round-trip cost is **D1**.
  - **TAI-02 / TAE-01** fixed: the budget is hard for authorisation (only closes and stop repair pass at or over it); A3 refreshes a stale token and detaches on any other auth refusal; the reconnector waits out a spent window (`BUDGET_WAIT_S`) instead of dying; the attach retries in-process with full-jitter backoff, then exits 4; refusals exit 2 or 3, which the unit does not restart; `StartLimitBurst=10` per hour. Proof: `test_the_budget_is_hard_for_authorisation_but_never_for_a_close`, `test_a_reconnect_in_the_close_reserve_waits_for_the_window_and_lives`, `test_an_application_refused_on_reconnect_detaches_and_ends_the_task`, `test_a_stale_token_at_the_grant_is_refreshed_once`. The audit's per-24 h connect ceiling is not built: the hard budget bounds connects instead, and A9 counting them is **D1**.
  - **TAI-03** mitigated: the watchdog never claims a mock flatten while the heartbeat reports practice or live (`WATCHDOG_PLUGIN_MISMATCH`, outcome `flatten_failed`; `test_the_mock_plugin_never_claims_to_flatten_a_venue_account`). The real cTrader watchdog is GAP-P12D-131 (i).
  - **TAI-04 / TAE-10** fixed: `ensure_stop` is total — any venue error falls through to a close, the open's report always reaches Risk, a failed close is logged critical, and the periodic reconcile flattens an ACIS position the venue shows without a stop (`test_a_refused_stop_amend_closes_the_position_and_still_reports_the_fill`, `test_a_stop_that_can_be_neither_rested_nor_closed_is_reported_critical`).
  - **TAI-05** fixed: at a restart Risk is built from venue truth and the fills SYNCING recovered are discarded, never replayed on top (`test_a_stop_that_fired_while_down_leaves_no_phantom_after_a_restart`). Its sibling, found in triage — a PAPER process adopted the demo account's positions and ingested its fills — is fixed too: Risk's book is fixed per process by its Risk store (`test_a_paper_process_leaves_the_accounts_positions_and_fills_out_of_its_book`).
  - **TAI-06** fixed: the periodic reconcile runs in practice only (`test_the_periodic_reconcile_and_token_refresh_run_in_practice_only`).
  - **TAI-07** fixed: `--resume` lifts the persisted halts at the terminal through ACIS-08's `acis_resume`, the typed token checked against `ACIS_HUMAN_TOKEN_SHA256` (`test_a_human_resume_at_the_terminal_lifts_the_persisted_halts`, `test_a_wrong_token_resumes_nothing`).
  - **TAI-08** fixed: an ack timeout is a `ConnectionError`, so the orchestrator reconciles and the face searches before any resend (`test_an_ack_timeout_through_the_orchestrator_is_searched_and_reported_once`).
  - **TAI-09** fixed: a close the venue refuses is marked NOT_SENT and returned as a report, never an escaping error (`test_a_close_the_venue_refuses_is_a_report_and_leaves_nothing_in_flight`); this also covers TAE-09's `POSITION_NOT_OPEN` race.
  - **TAI-10** fixed at every start: a foreign position refuses the start, exit 2 (`test_a_foreign_position_at_a_later_start_refuses_it`, which also found `attach_venue` opening `data/runtime` before it existed — fixed). One that appears mid-run is GAP-P12D-131 (k).
  - **TAI-11** fixed: one engine per deployment root (`test_a_second_engine_on_one_root_is_refused`); the meter counts from the shared ledger, so a probe and the engine spend one budget; the runbook stops the unit before a terminal session (`deploy/README.md`).
  - **TAI-12** fixed: no order leaves a link that is not READY (`test_no_order_leaves_a_link_that_is_re_syncing`); a reconnect's missed deals reach Risk as venue fills at SYNCING, and the next periodic reconcile compares positions.
  - **TAI-13 / TAE-04** fixed: no tick processed for `fast_loop_stall_s` while a market is open is a stall — flatten, halt, exit 1 (`test_a_fast_loop_silent_while_a_market_is_open_is_flattened_and_halted`). The watchdog still attests only the heartbeat's freshness; a blocked event loop stops the heartbeat itself, so the one case it could not see (a live Risk timer over a hung Loop 3) is now the runtime's own stall.
  - **TAI-14** fixed: practice keeps its own Risk store (`test_practice_keeps_its_own_risk_store`), and is entered before Risk's first evaluation.
  - **TAI-15** mitigated: an adopted position carries the venue's volume-weighted entry price and its stop (`test_an_adopted_position_carries_the_venues_entry_price_and_stop`); target, group and `opened_at` are GAP-P12D-131 (j).
  - **TAI-16 / TAE-07** fixed: history paging is complete whatever order the venue pages in (`test_history_paging_is_complete_in_either_order`).
  - **TAI-17** fixed: the token is refreshed at the quiet reconcile moment, in practice (`test_the_periodic_reconcile_and_token_refresh_run_in_practice_only`).
  - **TAI-18** fixed and declared: the minimum lot is injected in paper and practice only (`test_the_minimum_lot_is_injected_in_paper_and_practice_only`); the policy's three departures from the book are declared in `core/runtime/policy.py` and owned by [[GAP-P12D-118]].
  - **TAI-19** fixed: GAP-P12D-69/70 closed with residuals; the reconnect docstring, the unit's comment, the `deploy/README.md` compose line and the `equity_poll_s` comment corrected; GAP-P12D-126 (c) annotated; every finding owned here.
  - **TAE-02** fixed in code: an account disconnect is a full reconnect with SYNCING and re-subscribed spots (`test_an_account_disconnect_is_a_full_reconnect_with_sync_and_spots`). The spec text is **D4**.
  - **TAE-03 / TAE-11** fixed: liveness 60 s (twice the server's heartbeat), the session takes the declared cadences, and history requests are paced below the documented 5 a second (`test_the_session_takes_the_declared_cadences`, `test_history_requests_are_paced_below_the_documented_rate`).
  - **TAE-05** is **D3**.
  - **TAE-06** fixed: a maintenance end is seconds on `ProtoOAErrorRes` and milliseconds on `ProtoErrorRes` (`test_maintenance_end_is_seconds_on_oa_error_res_and_milliseconds_on_error_res`).
  - **TAE-08** fixed: an unroutable event is logged critical and skipped, and the runtime supervises the consumer (`test_an_unknown_event_enum_does_not_silence_later_venue_fills`).
  - **TAE-09** fixed in part: A3 (with TAI-02), the `retryAfter` pause, `POSITION_NOT_OPEN` (with TAI-09), the proactive refresh (TAI-17). The bounded retries for `PENDING_EXECUTION`/`POSITION_LOCKED` and the §12 table as a code map are GAP-P12D-131 (l).
  - **TAE-12** is **D4**.
  - **TAE-13** fixed: the heartbeat goes out one poll early, so no gap exceeds 10 s (`test_the_heartbeat_goes_out_before_its_interval_ever_passes`; `test_the_heartbeat_is_the_strictest_source_and_liveness_is_bounded` updated to the new threshold).
  - **TAE-14** is GAP-P12D-131 (i), with TAI-03.
- Owner decisions pending (none blocks the first demo run; each is a locked-spec text or a policy the owner owns):
  - **D1 (TAI-01, TAI-02): A9's cost.** One round trip measures 4 requests (open 1; close = reconcile 2 + close 1), not A9's 2, so at 50 orders the real projection is 960 > 900. Amend ACIS-13 §6.5 A9 to the measured cost (and count connects), which lowers `max_orders_per_day` 50 → 35 at today's budget.
  - **D2 (TAI-01): the equity breaker in the close reserve.** The practice account poll is a `query` and is refused inside the reserve, so Risk judges the last poll there. Options: flatten-and-halt at the reserve edge; let the poll spend the reserve at a slower cadence; or accept it (the venue stops still rest).
  - **D3 (TAE-05): the stop trigger side.** cTrader triggers a long's stop on the bid; the harness and paper trigger on the mid, so practice stops hit up to a spread earlier. Options: send the stop so its bid trigger sits at the Governor's mid level (parity with what the harness grades); model trade-side triggering in the book and paper; or declare it. The demo account type (Standard, or Razor with a commission `instruments.yaml` declares as 0) is read from the venue's symbol at the first attach.
  - **D4 (TAE-02, TAE-12): two declarations.** ACIS-13 §8.1/§12: an account disconnect is a full reconnect (as built). And demo fills and costs never calibrate C8 or satisfy the operational-referent pass (Pepperstone does not charge demo accounts interest), so carry is excluded when practice P&L is compared with the harness.
- **OWNER RULED 2026-09-30 (one batch, each the recommended option):** D1 "Count 4, cap 35/day"; D2 "Flatten and halt" at the reserve edge; D3 "Widen the sent stop" so its bid/ask trigger sits at the Governor's mid level; D4 both declarations ratified ("Disconnect = full reconnect", "Demo costs never calibrate C8"). To apply: ACIS-13 v1.2 text (§6.3, §6.5, §8.1, §12, §4.2) and the code for D1–D3, each with a test.
- **APPLIED 2026-09-30** as ACIS-13 v1.2 (§4.2, §6.3, §6.5, §8.1, §12) and code: D1 `ROUND_TRIP = 4` in the A9 check, the `VenueConfig` validator, `max_orders_per_day: 35`; D2 `Runtime._reserve_edge` (practice book only, once per entry into the reserve); D3 `relative_stop(..., spread)` fed by `CTraderFeed.spread`, `VENUE_NO_QUOTE` without a quote; D4 the spec text for what was already built. Proof: `test_the_order_allowance_counts_four_requests_a_round_trip`, `test_reaching_the_close_reserve_in_practice_flattens_and_halts_once`, `test_the_sent_stop_is_widened_by_the_live_spread`, `test_no_live_quote_means_no_open`, `test_an_account_disconnect_is_a_full_reconnect_with_sync_and_spots`.
- Justification: fixed. Every change is stricter or at parity with the harness: fewer orders a day, a flatten-and-halt where the breaker would be blind, stops placed where the graded mid trigger sits; no guardrail value, equity floor or resume path changed (D2 adds a halt trigger, lifted only by the human resume).
- Residual: GAP-P12D-131 — items (h)–(l) own what this audit left: the dead-link-mid-order runtime test, the cTrader watchdog config and ACIS-13 §14.2 case 8, adopted-position metadata, mid-run foreign positions, and the §12 error table as a code map.
- Linked: [[GAP-P12D-131]], [[GAP-0020]], [[GAP-0075]], [[GAP-0099]], [[GAP-0091b]], [[GAP-P12D-126]], [[GAP-P12D-69]], [[GAP-P12D-70]], [[GAP-P12D-118]]

## GAP-P12D-131: what the three-loop runtime still owes before an unattended demo — found building it, each item bounded today
